\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{i,2}=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\sigma_{s}dW_{s}-\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}W,\qquad\mathcal{E}_{i,3}=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\chi_{s}dJ_{s}^{\infty}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}.

The following estimates, established in Lemma
8
of
D
,
are often used:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\left|\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}\right|^{p}\right]\leq C\begin{cases}h^{p},&\ell=1,~p>0\\
h^{\frac{(2\wedge p)+p}{2},}&\ell=2,~p>0\\
h^{1+\frac{p}{2}}&\ell=3,~p\in[1,\infty)\cap(Y,\infty).\end{cases}

(35)

In particular
\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\left|\mathcal{E}_{i}\right|^{p}\right]\leq Ch^{1+\frac{p}{2}}
for all
p\geq 2
.
By our expansion for the truncated second-order moment of Lévy process given in Proposition
4
of
C
,
we can easily see that

\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]=\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}h+\widehat{A}_{i}(\varepsilon,h)h+o_{P}(h^{\frac{3}{2}}),

because the higher-order terms
h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}
,
h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}
, and
h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}}
(
\bar{\delta}>0
arbitrary) are smaller than
h^{\frac{3}{2}}
due to the restrictions
Y<8/5
and
h_{n}^{\frac{3}{2(2+Y)}\wedge\frac{1}{2}}\ll\varepsilon_{n}\ll h_{n}^{\frac{1}{4-s}}
.
Therefore, for (
33
) to hold, it suffices that

\displaystyle\quad{\mathcal{R}}_{i}:=\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]=o_{P}(h^{3/2}).

(36)

Clearly,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2|\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon,|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]|

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon<|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon,|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]=\sum_{\ell=1}^{5}\mathcal{R}_{i,\ell}.

The terms
\mathcal{R}_{i,2}
and
\mathcal{R}_{i,5}
are straightforward since
\mathcal{R}_{i,\ell}\leq{}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\right]\leq Ch^{2}=o_{P}(h^{3/2})
. For the term
\mathcal{R}_{i,3}
, expanding the product
x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, we get 9 terms of the form
A_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:=|\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}x_{i,\ell^{\prime}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon,|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]|
(one for each pair
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\{1,2,3\})
. For terms with
x_{i,1}
, since
|x_{i,1}|\leq Kh
clearly

\displaystyle A_{\ell,1}

\displaystyle\leq{}Ch\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}|\right]\leq{}Ch\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq{}Ch^{2}.

For terms involving
x_{i,3}
, Lemma
17
of
F

implies

\displaystyle A_{\ell,3}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}||\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|^{2}|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}^{2}|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Ch^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{2-Y}{2}}h=o_{P}(h^{3/2}).

The terms involving
x_{i,2}
are more delicate. We start with

A_{\ell,2}\leq C|\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}\right]|+C\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W||\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon\,\text{ or }\,|x_{i}|>{}\varepsilon\}}\right].

(37)

Clearly,
\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}\right]=0
for
\ell=1,3
. For
\ell=2
,
\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W\mathcal{E}_{i,2}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}(\sigma_{s}-\sigma_{t_{i-1}})ds\right]=O_{P}(h^{2})
.
For the second term in (
37
) on the event
\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon\,\text{ or }\,|x_{i}|>{}\varepsilon\}
, we have that
|x_{i,\ell}|>\varepsilon/4
for at least one
\ell
or
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon/4
. The case
|x_{i,1}|>\varepsilon/4
is eventually impossible for
n
large enough (since
b
is bounded), while both cases
|x_{i,2}|>\varepsilon/4
and
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon/4
are straightforward to handle using the Markov’s and Hölder’s inequalities. For instance, for any
m\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}

\displaystyle\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W||\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}|{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon/4\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{m}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W||\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}||\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{m}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{m}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{3}\right]^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}|^{3}\right]^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{3m}\right]^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{m}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}h^{\frac{5}{6}}h^{\frac{1}{3}+\frac{m}{2}}=Ch^{\frac{1}{2}}h^{\frac{5}{6}}h^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\frac{h}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}.

(38)

So, by picking
m=1
, we can make this term
o_{P}(h^{3/2})
. The remaining term is when
|x_{i,3}|>\varepsilon/4
. In that case, for any
p,q>2
such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}
, applying Lemma
6
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W||\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i,3}|>{}\varepsilon/4\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}C\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{p}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{\frac{3}{2}}(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}}\ll h^{\frac{3}{2}},

since, by our assumption on
\varepsilon
, we have
h^{\frac{1}{Y}}\ll\varepsilon
. We then conclude that

\mathcal{R}_{i,3}=o_{P}(h^{\frac{3}{2}})
.

It remains to analyze
\mathcal{R}_{i,1}
and
\mathcal{R}_{i,4}
. The proof is similar in both cases and we only give the details for the second case to save space. For some
\delta=\delta_{n}\to{}0
(
0<\delta<\varepsilon
), whose precise asymptotic behavior will be determined below, we consider the decomposition:

\mathcal{R}_{i,4}\leq\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon-\delta<|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon-\delta,\varepsilon\leq|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\}}\right]=:\mathcal{D}_{i,1}+\mathcal{D}_{i,2}.

By the expansion
in Proposition
5
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{i,1}

\displaystyle=Ch[\varepsilon^{2-Y}-(\varepsilon-\delta)^{2-Y}]+C^{\prime}h^{2}[\varepsilon^{-Y}-(\varepsilon-\delta)^{-Y}]+o_{P}(h^{\frac{3}{2}})

\displaystyle=Ch\varepsilon^{1-Y}\delta+o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{1-Y}\delta)+o_{P}(h^{\frac{3}{2}}).

(39)

Therefore, to obtain
\mathcal{D}_{i,1}=o_{P}(h^{\frac{3}{2}})
, we require

\displaystyle\delta\ll h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{Y-1}.

(40)

For
\mathcal{D}_{i,2}
, note that
|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon-\delta
and
\varepsilon\leq|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|
imply that
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta
. Also,
\varepsilon\leq|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|
implies that
|x_{i,\ell}|>\varepsilon/4
for at least one
\ell
or
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon/4
. The case
|x_{i,1}|>\varepsilon/4
is eventually impossible for
n
large enough, while both cases
|x_{i,2}|>\varepsilon/4
and
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon/4
are again straightforward to handle using Markov’s and Hölder’s inequalities as in (
38
). Therefore, we need only to consider the case when
|x_{i,3}|>\varepsilon/4
and
\max\{|x_{i,1}|,|x_{i,2}|,|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\}\leq{}\varepsilon/4
. In particular, since
|x_{i}|=|x_{i,1}+x_{i,2}+x_{i,3}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|<\varepsilon
, we have
\varepsilon/4<|x_{i,3}|\leq{}C\varepsilon
for some
C
. Then, we are left to analyze the following term:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\leq{}C\varepsilon,|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}C\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i,\ell}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\leq{}C\varepsilon,|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta\}}\right]

\displaystyle=:\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\mathcal{V}_{i,\ell}.

Clearly,
\mathcal{V}_{i,1}=O_{P}(h^{2})=o_{P}(h^{3/2})
. For
\mathcal{V}_{i,2}
, by Hölder’s inequality, for any
p,q>1
,
r\geq 2
, such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}=1
, recalling (
35
) and Lemma
6
below,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{i,2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\}}|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}C\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2p}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{r}\right]^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\delta}h(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}}(h^{1+\frac{r}{2}})^{\frac{1}{r}}=\frac{C}{\delta}h^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{q}}.

If
Y<1
, we take
q\to\infty
and
p=r=2
, to conclude that we only need
\delta\gg h^{1/2}
for
\mathcal{V}_{i,2}=o_{P}(h^{3/2})
to hold. This is consistent with (
40
) (meaning they can be met simultaneously for at least one choice of the sequence
\delta
), since
Y<1
.

Now we consider the case
Y\in(1,8/5)
. Clearly

\mathcal{V}_{i,2}\leq C\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\}}(|\mathcal{E}_{i,1}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i,2}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i,3}|)\right].

Note
{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\}}\leq{\bf 1}_{\{K\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|\}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}

\displaystyle\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\}}|\mathcal{E}_{i,1}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq h\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(K\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}

\displaystyle\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\}}|\mathcal{E}_{i,2}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{4}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}|\mathcal{E}_{i,2}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}\left(K\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y}.

For
\ell=3
, with
p,q>1
,
Y<r<2
, such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}=1
, from (
35
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\}}|\mathcal{E}_{i,3}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq Ch(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}}(h^{1+\frac{r}{2}})^{\frac{1}{r}}=Ch^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{q}}.

Then, taking
r
close to
Y
,
p
large, and
q
close to
Y/(Y-1)
, we obtain, for some
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}>0
that can be made arbitrarily small,

{\mathcal{V}}_{i,2}\leq\frac{C}{\delta}\left(h^{\frac{5}{2}-s^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{1-Y-s^{\prime\prime}}+h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y}\right)=O\left(\delta^{-1}h^{\frac{5}{2}-s^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{1-Y-s^{\prime\prime}}\right),

where we used
h^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll\varepsilon
to conclude that
h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y}\ll h^{\frac{5}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-Y}\ll h^{\frac{5}{2}-s^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{1-Y-s^{\prime\prime}}.

Thus, for
\mathcal{V}_{i,2}=o_{P}(h^{3/2})
to hold, it suffices that for some appropriately small
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle h^{1-s^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{1-Y-s^{\prime\prime}}\ll\delta.

(41)

The conditions (
40
) and (
41
) are consistent,
since

\displaystyle h^{1-s^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{1-Y-s^{\prime\prime}}\ll h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{Y-1}\;\Longleftrightarrow\;h^{\frac{1-2s^{\prime}}{4Y-4+2s^{\prime\prime}}}\ll\varepsilon,\;

(42)

which is implied by our condition
\varepsilon\gg h^{\frac{3}{2(2+Y)}}

when provided
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}
are both chosen small enough, since
\frac{3}{2(2+Y)}<\frac{1}{4-4Y}
when
Y<8/5
.

It remains to analyze
\mathcal{V}_{i,3}
.
As a consequence of Lemma
9
of
C

7
7

7

This estimate is shaper than what can be obtained by applying simply Hölder’s inequality and, hence, require some special handling
, provided that the condition
\delta\gg h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{Y/2}
holds,
we have

\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{V}}_{i,3}\leq C\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty})^{2}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|\leq\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta_{n}\}}]=O(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y})=o(h^{3/2}),

where the second equality follows from
\varepsilon\gg h^{\frac{3}{2(2+Y)}}
. The condition
\delta\gg h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{Y/2}
can be met under (
40
) since
h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{Y/2}\ll h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{Y-1}
. Thus, (
36
) holds and this concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3
.

Let
\widehat{A}_{i}(\varepsilon,h)
be as in (
32
)
and suppose that
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,\frac{8}{5})
and
{h_{n}^{\frac{3}{2(2+Y)}\wedge\frac{1}{2}}}\ll\varepsilon_{n}\ll h_{n}^{\frac{1}{4-Y}}
. Then, for any
i=1,\dots,n
and
\zeta>1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\widehat{A}_{i}(\zeta\varepsilon,h)h-\widehat{A}_{i}(\varepsilon,h)h+o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{4-Y}{2}}\right).

(43)

Proof.

We use the same notation as in (
98
). From
the expansion
in Proposition
4
of
B
,
we have that

\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]=\widehat{A}_{i}(\zeta\varepsilon,h)h-\widehat{A}_{i}(\varepsilon,h)h+o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{4-Y}{2}}\right),

since all higher-order terms
h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}
,
h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}
,
h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-\frac{Y}{2}}
, and
h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}}
are all
o(h\varepsilon^{\frac{4-Y}{2}})
,
for
\bar{\delta}
small enough.
Therefore, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{i}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq|x_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=o_{P}\Big(h\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{4-Y}{2}}\Big).

(44)

We have the decomposition:

\displaystyle|\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon,|x_{i}|>\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon,|x_{i}|<\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+4\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}|{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon,\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon,|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon,|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|<\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle=:\sum_{\ell=1}^{6}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{i,\ell}.

The term
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{i,3}
is clearly
O_{P}(h^{2})
and hence,
o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{(4-Y)/2})
.
For
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{i,4}
, by Cauchy’s inequality, the expansion
in Proposition
4
of
B

and (
35
),

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{i,4}\leq{}2\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}|{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}C\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(h\varepsilon^{2-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}(h^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}=h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{2-Y}{2}},

which is
o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{(4-Y)/2})
since
h^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll\varepsilon
. The proofs of the remaining terms are similar. We give only one of those for simplicity. Consider
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{i,1}
. We decompose it as

\displaystyle(1/2)\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{i,1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\zeta\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon+\delta\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon,|x_{i}|>\zeta\varepsilon+\delta\}}\right]

\displaystyle=:\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{i,1}+\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{i,2}.

By the
expansion in
Proposition
4
of
B
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{i,1}

\displaystyle=Ch[(\zeta\varepsilon)^{2-Y}-(\zeta\varepsilon-\delta)^{2-Y}]+C^{\prime}h^{2}[(\zeta\varepsilon)^{-Y}-(\zeta\varepsilon-\delta)^{-Y}]+o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon^{\frac{4-Y}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=Ch\varepsilon^{1-Y}\delta+o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{1-Y}\delta)+o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon^{\frac{4-Y}{2}}\right).

Therefore, to obtain
\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{i,1}=o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{(4-Y)/2})
, we require

\displaystyle\delta\ll\varepsilon^{1+\frac{Y}{2}}.

(45)

For
\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{i,2}
, we follow the same
analysis as in the proof of Lemma
2
. Indeed, the arguments following expression (
40
) show that under the condition

\displaystyle\delta\gg h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{Y/2},

(46)

we have

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{i,2}=O_{P}\left(\delta^{-1}h^{\frac{5}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-Y}\right)+O_{P}(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y})+o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{4-Y}{2}}\right).

(47)

Observe the condition (
46
) is consistent with (
45
) since
h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{Y/2}\ll\varepsilon^{1+Y/2}\iff h^{1/2}\ll\varepsilon
.
For the first term on the right-hand side of (
47
) to be
o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{(4-Y)/2})
, we require
h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{-1-\frac{Y}{2}}\ll\delta
, which is consistent with the condition (
45
) since
h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{-1-\frac{Y}{2}}\ll\varepsilon^{1+\frac{Y}{2}}
under
\varepsilon\gg h^{\frac{3}{2(2+Y)}}
.
Therefore taking any
\delta\to 0
such that
(h^{1/2}\varepsilon\ \vee\ h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{-1-\frac{Y}{2}})\ll\delta\ll\varepsilon^{1+Y/2}
, we obtain
\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{i,2}=o_{P}\Big(h\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{4-Y}{2}}\Big)
, which establishes (
44
) and completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4
.

Suppose that
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon\ll{}h^{\frac{1}{4-Y}}
and
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,8/5)
. Then, for any
k\geq{}2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\left(\Delta_{i}^{n}{X^{\prime}}\right)^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}{X^{\prime}}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right]

(48)

\displaystyle\quad=(2k-1)!!\,\sigma^{2k}_{t_{i-1}}h^{k}+\frac{\bar{C}|\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{Y}}{2k-Y}\,h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}+o_{P}\big(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}\big)+O_{P}\big(h^{k+\frac{1}{2}}\big).

Proof.

We use the same notation as in (
98
). The proof is similar to that of Lemma
2
. From the expansion
of Proposition
5
,
under our assumptions, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon}\}}\right]=d_{1}\sigma^{2k}_{t_{i-1}}h^{k}+d_{2}h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}+o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}\right),

(49)

where
d_{1}=(2k-1)!!
and
d_{2}=\frac{\bar{C}}{2k-Y}|\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{Y}
.
Therefore, for (
48
) to hold, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle=o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}\right).

(50)

Consider the decomposition:

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\}}\right]+C\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon<|x_{i}|\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=0}^{2k-1}\binom{2k}{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\left|x_{i}^{\ell}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2k-\ell}\right|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon,|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon<|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\}}\right]=\sum_{m=1}^{4}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i,m}.

By (
35
), the term
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i,2}=O_{P}(h^{1+k})
and, thus, is
o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y})
.
In light of (
49
), the
\ell
–th summand appearing in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i,3}
is bounded by a constant times

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\left|x_{i}^{\ell}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2k-\ell}\right|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\left|x_{i}\right|^{2\ell}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\left|\mathcal{E}_{i}\right|^{4k-2\ell}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(O_{P}(h^{\frac{\ell}{2}})+O_{P}(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{2\ell-Y}{2}})\right)(h^{1+2k-\ell})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\big(h^{k+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+O_{P}\big(h^{1+k-\frac{\ell}{2}}\varepsilon^{\ell-\frac{Y}{2}}\big).

The second term above is
o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y})
when
\varepsilon\gg\sqrt{h}
.

It remains to analyze
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i,1}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i,4}
. The proof is similar in both cases and we only give the details for the second case to save space. For some
\delta\to{}0
(
0<\delta<\varepsilon
), whose precise asymptotic behavior will be determined below, we consider the decomposition:

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i,4}\leq\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon-\delta<|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon-\delta,\varepsilon\leq|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\}}\right]=:\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{i,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{i,2}.

By the expansion of Proposition
5
,
we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{i,1}

\displaystyle=Ch[\varepsilon^{2k-Y}-(\varepsilon-\delta)^{2k-Y}]+o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y}\delta)+o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}\right).

Thus, to obtain
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{i,1}=o_{P}\left(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}\right)
, we require

\displaystyle\delta\ll\varepsilon.

(51)

As in the proof of Lemma
2
, when dealing with
\mathcal{D}_{i,2}
, it suffices to analyze the term:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}||\leq{}C\varepsilon,|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}C\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i,\ell}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}||\leq{}C\varepsilon,|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta\}}\right]

\displaystyle=:\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,\ell}.

Clearly,
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,1}=O_{P}(h^{2k})=o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y})
. For
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,2}
, by Hölder’s inequality, for any
p,q>1
and
r>Y
such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}=1
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\}}|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}C\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)^{2kp}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[\frac{\varepsilon}{4}<|x_{i,3}|\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{r}\right]^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\delta}h^{k}(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}}(h^{1+\frac{r}{2}})^{\frac{1}{r}}=\frac{C}{\delta}h^{k+\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{q}}.

For the above to be smaller than
h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}
, we need
\delta\gg h^{k+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\varepsilon^{Y-2k-\frac{Y}{q}}
. For
\delta
to be consistent with (
51
), we need that
h^{k+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\varepsilon^{Y-2k-\frac{Y}{q}-1}\ll 1
. This is always possible if we take
q
close to
1
,
p\nearrow\infty
, and
r\nearrow\infty
because
h^{k+\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-2k-1}\ll 1
under our condition
\varepsilon\gg\sqrt{h}
.

It remains to analyze
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,3}
. Under the additional constraint

\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{Y/2}\ll\delta,

(52)

Lemma
9
of
C

implies

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,3}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|\leq{}C\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta_{n}\}}\right]=O(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-2Y}),

which implies
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,3}=o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y})
since
h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-2Y}\ll h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}
under our condition
\varepsilon\gg h^{1/2}
. The conditions (
51
) and (
52
) are consistent since
h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{Y/2}\ll\varepsilon
, under our conditions.
Thus, we conclude that
(
50
) holds, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5
.

Provided
h_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\ll\varepsilon_{n}\ll h_{n}^{\frac{1}{4-Y}}
for some
s\in(0,1/2)
, and
r_{0}\vee r_{1}\in(0,Y\wedge 1)
, for any
\alpha\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\big|\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon\}}-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big|

(53)

\displaystyle=O_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-r_{1}})+O_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-\alpha r_{0}})+O_{P}(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-r_{1}\alpha})+O_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y-1+\alpha}).

In particular, when
h_{n}^{\frac{4}{8+Y}}\ll\varepsilon_{n}\ll h_{n}^{\frac{1}{4-Y}},
with
r_{0},r_{1}
as in Assumption
2
,

\big|\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon\}}-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big|=o_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2-Y/2}),

(54)

and the estimates (
33
), (
43
) and (
48
) hold with
X
in place of
X^{\prime}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{2k}:=\big|\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon\}}-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big|
and
r=r_{0}\vee r_{1}
.
Let us recall the definition of
J^{0}
and
X^{\prime}
in (
102
) and (
31
), respectively.
Write
V_{t}=X_{t}-X^{\prime}_{t}=X_{t}^{j,0}+\int_{0}^{t}\chi_{s}dJ_{s}^{0}
, and set

X_{t}^{j,0}=\int_{0}^{t}\int\delta_{0}(s,{z})\mathfrak{p}_{0}(ds,dz)+\int_{0}^{t}\int\delta_{1}(s,{z})\mathfrak{p}_{1}(ds,dz)=:Y_{t}^{0}+Y_{t}^{1}.

For any fixed integer
k\geq 1
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2k}

\displaystyle=\big|(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2k}-(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}\big|{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon,~|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon,~|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|>\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|>\varepsilon,~|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle=:T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}.

A Taylor expansion of
(x^{\prime}+v)^{2k}
at
v=0
gives
|(x^{\prime}+v)^{2k}-(x^{\prime})^{2k}|\leq K|v|\big(|x^{\prime}|^{2k-1}+|v|^{2k-1}\big)
, so

T_{1}\leq K\big(|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|^{2k}+|\Delta_{i}^{n}V||\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|^{2k-1}\big){\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon,~|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon\}.}

(55)

Note that Corollary 2.1.9 in
Jacod and Protter 2011
implies that for each
p\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{m}|}{\varepsilon}\wedge 1\bigg)^{p}\leq Kh\varepsilon^{-r_{m}},\quad m=0,1.

(56)

Also, since
J^{0}
is compound Poisson, writing
N^{0}(ds,dx)
for the jump measure of
J^{0}
,

\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\chi_{s}dJ^{0}_{s}\Big|\wedge 1\Big)^{p}\leq\mathbb{P}\big(N^{0}([t_{i-1},t_{i}],\mathbb{R})>0\big)\leq Kh.

Thus we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left(\left|\Delta_{i}^{n}V\right|^{p}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)\leq Kh\varepsilon^{p-r}.

Therefore, since
|x+v|\leq\varepsilon
and
|x|\leq\varepsilon
imply
|v|\leq 2\varepsilon
, from (
55
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq K\Big(\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}V)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}+\varepsilon^{2k-1}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq Kh\varepsilon^{2k-r}.

On
T_{2}
, write

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon,~|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|>\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}\}}+(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}\}}

\displaystyle=:T_{2}^{\prime}+T_{2}^{\prime\prime},

(57)

where
\alpha\geq 1
is to be later chosen.
Using that
|x^{\prime}+v|\leq\varepsilon
and
|x^{\prime}|>\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}
imply
|v|>\varepsilon^{\alpha}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}T_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq K\varepsilon^{2k}\mathbb{P}_{i-1}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|>\varepsilon^{\alpha},|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|>\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle\leq K\varepsilon^{2k}\Big(\mathbb{P}_{i-1}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}(V-Y^{1})|>\varepsilon^{\alpha}/2\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\big(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{1}|>\varepsilon^{\alpha}/2\big)\mathbb{P}\big(|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|>\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq K\varepsilon^{2k}\big(\varepsilon^{-\alpha r_{0}}h+h^{2}\varepsilon^{-Y-r_{1}\alpha}\big),

where we used that
\mathbb{P}(|\Delta X^{\prime}|>\varepsilon)\leq Kh\varepsilon^{-Y}
as a consequence of Lemma
6
and that
\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\big(|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{1}|>u\big)\leq\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\big(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}Y^{1}|}{u}\wedge 1\big)\leq hu^{-r_{1}}
per (
56
).

On the other hand, for
T^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, since
|x^{\prime}+v|\leq\varepsilon
and
|x^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}
give
|v|\leq 2\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}T_{2}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}\}}

\displaystyle\leq K\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\big[\big((\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}+(\Delta_{i}^{n}V)^{2k}\big){\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}\}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}\}}+O_{P}(\varepsilon^{2k-r}h).

Since
\alpha\geq 1
, arguing as in (
39
), by applying Lemma
4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha}\}}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\big(h\big((\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\alpha})^{2k-Y}-\varepsilon^{2k-Y}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=O_{P}(h\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y+\alpha}).

Now, turning to
T_{3},
write

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|>\varepsilon,~|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{\alpha}\}}+({\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}})^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{{\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{\alpha}<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon\}}}

\displaystyle=T_{3}^{\prime}+T_{3}^{\prime\prime}.

Since
|x^{\prime}+v|>\varepsilon
and
|x^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{\alpha}
imply
|v|>\varepsilon^{\alpha}
, the same arguments for
T_{2}^{\prime}
and
T_{2}^{\prime\prime}
apply to
T_{3}^{\prime},T_{3}^{\prime\prime}
, giving
(
53
).

To obtain (
54
) under
h_{n}^{\frac{4}{8+Y}}\ll\varepsilon_{n}\ll h_{n}^{\frac{1}{4-Y}}
, consider first
k=1
. In this case, we may simultaneously satisfy each of

\varepsilon^{2-\alpha r_{0}}h\ll h\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{2}}
,
(\!\iff\alpha<\frac{Y}{2r_{0}}
),
h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-Y-r_{1}\alpha}\ll h\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{2}}
(
\iff h^{\frac{2}{Y+2\alpha r_{1}}}\ll\varepsilon\;\Longleftarrow\varepsilon\gg h^{\frac{4}{8+Y}}
if
\alpha<\frac{16-2Y}{8r_{1}}
), and
h\varepsilon^{1-Y+\alpha}\ll h\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{2}}
(​
\iff\alpha>1+Y/2)
provided
\alpha
is chosen such that

1+\frac{Y}{2}<\alpha<\Big(\frac{Y}{2r_{0}}\Big)\wedge\Big(\frac{16-2Y}{8r_{1}}\Big),

which is always possible under the restrictions on
r_{0},r_{1}
in Assumption
2
. Since
r_{1}\leq Y/2
, we also have
\varepsilon^{2-r_{1}}\ll\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{2}}
, concluding (
54
) for
k=1
. When
k\geq 2
,
\varepsilon^{2k-\alpha r_{0}}h\ll h\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{2}}
,
h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-r_{1}\alpha}\ll h\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{2}}
, and
h\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y+\alpha}\ll h\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{2}}
all hold by taking
\alpha=1
.

Finally, since
\varepsilon\ll h^{\frac{1}{4-Y}}
implies
h\varepsilon^{2-Y/2}\ll h^{3/2}
, expression (
54
) implies each of (
33
), (
43
) and (
48
) hold with
X
in place of
X^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 6
.

Suppose
0<Y<2
, and
\varepsilon\to 0
with
\varepsilon\gg h^{1/Y}
. For all
p>1\vee Y
, the following estimates hold:

\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|}{\varepsilon}\wedge 1\right)^{p}\leq Kh\varepsilon^{-Y},\quad\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|}{\varepsilon}\wedge 1\right)^{p}\leq K\big(h\varepsilon^{-Y}+h^{p/2}\varepsilon^{-p}\big).

(58)

In particular, with
V=J
or
V=X
,

\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|>\varepsilon\right]\leq h\varepsilon^{-Y}.

Proof.

We only give the proof for
|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|
since the proof for other term is similar. It suffices to establish the bound for
\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}b_{t}dt
,
\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\sigma_{t}dW_{t}
and
\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{j}
separately. It holds immediately for
\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}b_{t}dt
, since
\varepsilon^{-p}\big|\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}b_{t}dt\big|^{p}\leq K\varepsilon^{-p}h^{p}\ll{\varepsilon^{-p}h^{p/2}}
. for the second term, Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality gives
\varepsilon^{-p}\mathbb{E}_{{i-1}}\big|\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\sigma_{t}dW_{t}|^{p}\leq K_{p}(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1})^{p}
.
For
\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{j}
, we first consider
\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{j,\infty}
as in (
6
).
Applying Corollary 2.1.9(a)
8
8

8

Strictly speaking, this corollary assumes
\varepsilon=h^{q}
for some
q\in(0,1/Y)
, though a straightforward adaptation shows it holds provided
h^{1/Y}\ll\varepsilon\ll 1
.
in, we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{{i-1}}\left(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{j,\infty}|}{\varepsilon}\wedge 1\right)^{p}\leq Kh\varepsilon^{-Y}.

Applying Corollary 2.1.9(c) in
Jacod and Protter 2011
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{{i-1}}\left(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{j,0}|}{\varepsilon}\wedge 1\right)^{p}\leq Kh\varepsilon^{-r},

where
r=r_{0}\vee r_{1}
, which gives

\mathbb{E}_{{i-1}}\left(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{j}|}{\varepsilon}\wedge 1\right)^{p}\leq Kh\varepsilon^{-Y}.

The remaining statements follow since

\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|>\varepsilon\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{{i-1}}\left(\frac{|\Delta_{i}^{n}V|}{\varepsilon}\wedge 1\right)^{p},

for every
p>0
, and
h^{p/2}\varepsilon^{-p}\ll h\varepsilon^{-Y}
for
p
large enough. This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Expansions for truncated moments of Lévy processes

In this section, we obtain some new high-order asymptotic expansions for the truncated moments
\mathbb{E}|\widehat{X}_{h_{n}}|^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h_{n}}|\leq\varepsilon_{n}\}}
of the Lévy process

\displaystyle\widehat{X}_{t}:=\sigma W_{t}+J_{t},

(59)

for a constant volatility value
\sigma\in[0,\infty)
, where, unless otherwise stated,
h_{n}
and
\varepsilon_{n}
satisfies

\varepsilon_{n}\to 0\quad\text{ and }\quad h_{n}\to{}0\quad\text{such that}\quad\varepsilon_{n}\gg\sqrt{h}_{n}.

(60)

For notational simplicity, we often omit the subscript
n
in
h_{n}
and
\varepsilon_{n}
.

Recall that here
J
is a Lévy process having a Lévy density of the form (
7
) with a function
q
satisfying Assumption
1
.
As a first step, we show our results hold under the following additional conditions (iii)-(v), below. We later show these additional conditions can, in fact, be dropped (Lemma
7
, below).

Assumption 3
.

(iii)

\displaystyle{\limsup_{|x|\rightarrow\infty}\frac{|\ln q(x)|}{|x|^{p}}<\infty}
, for some
p<Y\wedge 1
;

(iv)

for any
\varepsilon>0
,
\displaystyle{\inf_{|x|<\varepsilon}q(x)>0}
;

(v)

\displaystyle{\int_{|x|>1}q(x)|x|^{r}dx<\infty}
, for any
r>0
.

The conditions (i)-(v) of Assumptions
1
and
3
imply that

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}}\left(q(x)^{-1/2}-1\right)^{2}\nu(dx)<\infty,

which, according to Theorem 33.1 in
Sato 1999
, is sufficient for the existence of a measure
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
, locally equivalent to
\mathbb{P}
, so that, under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
,

W
is still a standard Brownian motion independent of
J
, but
J
is now a stable Lévy process with Lévy triplet
(\tilde{b},0,\tilde{\nu})
, where
\widetilde{\nu}(dx)
is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}(dx):=\big(C_{+}{\bf 1}_{(0,\infty)}(x)+C_{-}{\bf 1}_{(-\infty,0)}(x)\big)|x|^{-Y-1}dx,

(61)

and
\widetilde{b}:=b+\int_{0<|x|\leq 1}x(\tilde{\nu}-\nu)(dx)
.
More specifically (see
Figueroa-López et al. 2016
), for any
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\frac{d\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\big|_{\mathscr{F}_{t}}}{d\mathbb{P}\big|_{\mathscr{F}_{t}}}=e^{U_{t}},

(62)

with
U
given by

U_{t}:=\widetilde{U}_{t}+\eta t:=\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}}\varphi(x)\widetilde{N}(ds,dx)+\eta t,

(63)

where
\varphi(x)=-\ln q(x)
,
\eta:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}}\big(e^{-\varphi(x)}-1+\varphi(x)\big)\widetilde{\nu}(dx)
,
N(dt,dx)
is the jump measure of the process
J
, and
\widetilde{N}(dt,dx):=N(dt,dx)-\widetilde{\nu}(dx)dt
is its compensated version under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
. The representation (
63
) (including the
\eta
appearing in (
63
)) is well-defined under the conditions (i)-(v) of Assumptions
1
and
3
. We refer the reader to
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
for additional details.

Under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
, for an appropriate shift
\widetilde{\gamma}
, the process

S_{t}:=J_{t}-t\widetilde{\gamma}

(64)

is a strictly
Y
-stable process with location parameter 0 and its scale and skewness parameters given by
[(C_{+}+C_{-})\Gamma(-Y)|\cos(\pi Y/2)|]^{1/Y}
,
(C_{+}-C_{-})/(C_{+}+C_{-})
, respectively. With slight abuse of notation, for convenience we assume
S
has the same distribution under both probability measures
\mathbb{P}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
.

We first study the truncated moments of the Lévy process

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}_{t}:=\sigma W_{t}+S_{t},

(65)

and subsequently extend our results to the more general Lévy process
\widehat{X}_{t}=\sigma W_{t}+J_{t}
, first with
J
satisfying all the conditions (i)-(v) of Assumptions
1
and
3
, and then with only conditions (i)-(ii).

The following result is a refinement of Theorem 3.1 in
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
.

Proposition 3
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
, and
\varepsilon\to 0
with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon
. As
h=1/n\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{X}_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\widetilde{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}h+\widetilde{A}(\varepsilon,h)h+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)

(66)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+{O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)},

where
\widetilde{A}(\varepsilon,h)
is defined as:

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}(\varepsilon,h):=\frac{C_{+}+C_{-}}{2-Y}\varepsilon^{2-Y}-(C_{+}+C_{-})\frac{(Y+1)(Y+2)}{2Y}\sigma^{2}h\varepsilon^{-Y}.

(67)

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we set
\bar{C}:=C_{+}+C_{-}
. We may write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(\widetilde{X}_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\widetilde{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\!

(68)

\displaystyle\quad+2\sigma\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{h}S_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\!+\!\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big).

The sum of the first two terms of (
68
) is denoted
\mathcal{T}_{1,n}
.
By symmetry of
W_{1}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}h-\sigma^{2}h{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(W_{1}^{2}[{\bf 1}_{\{\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+S_{h}>\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}-S_{h}>\varepsilon\}}]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\sigma\sqrt{h}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{1}S_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}h-\sigma\sqrt{h}\varepsilon\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon+S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)+\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon-S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

(69)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\sigma\sqrt{h}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(S_{h}\bigg(\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon+S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)-\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon-S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\sigma^{2}h\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon+S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)+\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon-S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right),

where in the last equality we condition on
S_{h}
and apply the identities
{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(W_{1}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{W_{1}>x\}}\right)=x\phi(x)+\overline{\Phi}(x)
and
{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(W_{1}{\bf 1}_{\{x_{1}<W_{1}<x_{2}\}}\right)=\phi(x_{1})-\phi(x_{2})
.
From (
158
), we have
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\phi\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm{S_{h}}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)=\frac{\sigma\sqrt{h}}{2\pi}\mathcal{I}(0)
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left(\mp S_{h}\right)\,\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon\frac{\sigma\sqrt{h}}{2\pi}\left(\mathcal{I}(0)+(-1)^{1}\,2\left(i\varepsilon\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}h}{2}\right)\mathcal{I}(1)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\phi\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)+O\left(h^{5/2}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+O\left(\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h^{\frac{4-Y}{2Y}}e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\right),

(70)

where in the last equality we used (
152
). Therefore, plugging (
70
) into (
69
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}h-2\sigma\sqrt{h}\,\varepsilon\,{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon-S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)+\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon+S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\sigma^{2}h\,{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon-S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)+\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon+S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+O\bigg(\varepsilon\sqrt{h}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\bigg)+O\left(h^{\frac{2}{Y}}e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\right).

(71)

By Lemma
13
,

\displaystyle\quad{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle={{C}_{\pm}\sigma h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{-Y-1}+O\left(h^{\frac{5}{2}}\varepsilon^{-Y-3}\right)+O\bigg(e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\bigg).}

(72)

Also, by Lemma
18
, we have

\displaystyle\qquad{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(\overline{\Phi}\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\mp}}{Y}h\varepsilon^{-Y}+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}\right)+{O\left(h\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)}

(73)

\displaystyle\quad+{O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

Finally, we combine (
71
), (
72
), (
73
), Lemma
16
, and (
68
) to accomplish the proof.
∎

The following result gives an expansion for the truncated second moment of a Lévy process with a tempered stable jump component.

Proposition 4
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
, and
\varepsilon\to 0
with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon
.
Let
\widehat{X}
and
\widetilde{X}
be defined as in (
59
) and (
65
), and suppose that
J
is a Lévy process having a Lévy density of the form (
7
) with
q
satisfying conditions (i)-(v) of Assumptions
1
and
3
. Then, as
h\rightarrow 0
, for any fixed
\bar{\delta}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\widehat{X}_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{X}_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\widetilde{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+O\big(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-\frac{Y}{2}}\big)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)+O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}})

(74)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+{O\left(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

In particular, in light of (
66
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\widehat{X}_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}h+\widetilde{A}(\varepsilon,h)h

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+O\big(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-\frac{Y}{2}}\big)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)

(75)

\displaystyle\quad+O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}})+O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+{O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

Proof.

We set
\bar{C}:=C_{+}+C_{-}
and again consider the simple decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\widehat{X}_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|X_{h}^{2}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(A_{n}(\sigma W_{h},J_{h})\Big)+2\mathbb{E}\Big(B_{n}(\sigma W_{h},J_{h})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big(C_{n}(\sigma W_{h},J_{h})\Big),

(76)

where
A_{n}(w,s)=w^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|w+s|\leq{}\varepsilon_{n}\}}
,
B_{n}(w,s)=ws{\bf 1}_{\{|w+s|\leq{}\varepsilon_{n}\}}
, and

C_{n}(w,s)=s^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|w+s|\leq{}\varepsilon_{n}\}}
.
We prove the result in two steps.

Step 1.
We first analyze the difference between each of the first two terms in (
76
) and their counterparts under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
. By (
63
) and (
64
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n,1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}(A_{n}(\sigma W_{h},J_{h}))-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(A_{n}(\sigma W_{h},S_{h}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\big\{\mathbb{E}(B_{n}(\sigma W_{h},J_{h}))-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(B_{n}(\sigma W_{h},S_{h}))\big\}

(77)

can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n,1}

\displaystyle=e^{-\eta h}\left(-\sigma^{2}h\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{1}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}\Big)+2\sigma\sqrt{h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{1}J_{h}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(-\sigma^{2}h{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(W_{1}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+S_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}\Big)+2\sigma\sqrt{h}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{1}S_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sigma^{2}he^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\Big)W_{1}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sigma\sqrt{h}\,e^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\Big)W_{1}J_{h}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=:e^{-\eta h}\,I_{1}(h)-I_{2}(h)-\sigma^{2}he^{-\eta h}I_{3}(h)+2\sigma\sqrt{h}e^{-\eta h}\,I_{4}(h).

(78)

Note that, written in terms of the term
\mathcal{T}_{1,n}
of the proof of Proposition
3
,
I_{2}=-\sigma^{2}h+\mathcal{T}_{1,n}
. Therefore, combining (
71
)-(
73
), we get

\displaystyle I_{2}(h)

\displaystyle=-\bar{C}\frac{2Y+1}{Y}\sigma^{2}h^{2}\varepsilon^{-Y}+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+{O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad+{O\bigg(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\bigg)+O\left(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

For
I_{1}(h)
, first note that, as in (
69
), we have:

\displaystyle I_{1}(h)

\displaystyle=-\sigma\sqrt{h}\,\varepsilon\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon-J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)+\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon+J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sigma\sqrt{h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(J_{h}\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon-J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)-J_{h}\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon+J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

(79)

\displaystyle\quad-\sigma^{2}h\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon-J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)+\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon+J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right).

Recall that, under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
,
J_{t}=S_{t}+\tilde{\gamma}t
. Then, fixing
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}=\varepsilon\pm\tilde{\gamma}h
, we can apply (
72
) to deduce that:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\phi\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=C_{\pm}\sigma h^{\frac{3}{2}}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{-Y-1}+O\left(h^{\frac{5}{2}}\varepsilon^{-Y-3}\right)+O\bigg(e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\bigg).

(80)

Similarly, by (
70
) and (
73
),

\displaystyle\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\bigg(J_{h}\phi

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\bigg)

(81)

\displaystyle={\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(S_{h}\phi\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)\,+\,\tilde{\gamma}h\,{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(\phi\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle=\mp\varepsilon\,{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(\phi\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)+O\left(h^{\frac{5}{2}}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+O\bigg(\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\bigg),

and

\displaystyle~~~\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}\pm S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\mp}}{Y}h\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{-Y}+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}\right)+{O\left(h\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)}

(82)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon^{-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+{O\left(h^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right).}

Together, (
80
), (
81
), and (
82
) imply that
I_{1}(h)
has the following asymptotic behavior:

\displaystyle I_{1}(h)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2C_{+}Y+C_{-}}{Y}\sigma^{2}h^{2}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{+}^{-Y}-\frac{2C_{-}Y+C_{+}}{Y}\sigma^{2}h^{2}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{-}^{-Y}+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+{O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)}+O\bigg(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\bigg)+{O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

(83)

Therefore, we obtain that

\displaystyle e^{-\eta h}

\displaystyle\,I_{1}(h)-I_{2}(h)

\displaystyle=I_{1}(h)-I_{2}(h)+I_{1}(h)O\left(h\right)

\displaystyle=-\sigma^{2}h^{2}\frac{2C_{+}Y+C_{-}}{Y}\left(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{+}^{-Y}-\varepsilon^{-Y}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sigma^{2}h^{2}\frac{2C_{-}Y+C_{+}}{Y}\left(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{-}^{-Y}-\varepsilon^{-Y}\right)+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+{O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)}+O\Big(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\Big)+{O\Big(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\Big)}

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+{O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+O\Big(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\Big)+{O\left(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)},

(84)

where in the last equality we used that
h^{2}(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{-Y}-\varepsilon^{-Y})=O(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-1})
.
Next, using the proof in the Step 1.2 and Step 3 of Theorem 3.1 of
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
, we know, as
h\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle I_{3}(h)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\Big)W_{1}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}\Big)=o\big(h^{1/Y}\big),

(85)

\displaystyle I_{4}(h)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\Big)W_{1}J_{h}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)=O\big(h\varepsilon^{1-Y/2}\big).

(86)

Thus, combining (
78
), (
84
), (
85
), and (
86
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n,1}

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+{O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)}+O\big(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-\frac{Y}{2}}\big)+o\big(h^{1+\frac{1}{Y}}\big)

(87)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+{O\left(\sqrt{h}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

Step 2.
In this step, we will study the asymptotic behavior of the third term in (
76
), as
h\rightarrow 0
. By (
62
), (
63
)
and (
64
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n,2}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\Big(J_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+e^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\Big)S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\widetilde{\gamma}he^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}S_{h}{\bf 1}_{\{|W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\widetilde{\gamma}^{2}h^{2}e^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+O(h)\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=:I_{5,1}(h)+e^{-\eta h}I_{5,2}(h)+2\widetilde{\gamma}he^{-\eta h}I_{6}(h)+O\big(h^{2}\big)+O\Big(\varepsilon^{1-Y}h^{5/2}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\Big),

(88)

where in the last equality we used Lemma
16
.
We begin with the analysis of
I_{5,1}(h)
.
First note that, for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}[

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon-|\tilde{\gamma}|h\}}-{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{}I_{5,1}(h)\leq\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}[{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon+|\tilde{\gamma}|h\}}-{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}]\Big),

and, since, by Lemma
16
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\pm|\tilde{\gamma}|h\}}\Big)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{C}}{2-Y}h\left(\left(\varepsilon\pm|\widetilde{\gamma}|h\right)^{2-Y}-\varepsilon^{2-Y}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\bar{C}(1-Y)}{2}\sigma^{2}h^{2}\left(\left(\varepsilon\pm|\widetilde{\gamma}|h\right)^{-Y}-\varepsilon^{-Y}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}\right)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)+O\left(\varepsilon^{1-Y}h^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}\right)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)+O\left(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right),

we conclude that
I_{5,1}(h)=O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}\right)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)+O\big(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\big)
.
In
F
, expression (
213
), we show

\displaystyle I_{5,2}(h)=O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}}),

(89)

valid for all
\bar{\delta}>0
. Further, an application of Cauchy-Schwarz (c.f. Step 2.2 in
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
) and Lemma
16
together show
I_{6}(h)=O\big(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{1-Y/2}\big).
Thus,
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n,2}

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}\right)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)+O\left(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O(h\varepsilon^{3-Y}\big(h\varepsilon^{-Y}\big)^{-\delta})+O\big(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{1-Y/2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+O\big(h^{2}\big)+O\left(\varepsilon^{1-Y}h^{5/2}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}\right)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+{O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}})}+O\left(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

(90)

Finally, combining (
87
) and (
90
), we conclude the result.
∎

Next, we give an expansion for the truncated higher-order moments of
\widehat{X}
.

Proposition 5
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
, and
\varepsilon\to 0
with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon
.
Let
k
be a positive integer. Then, as
h\to 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\widehat{X}_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle=(2k-1)!!\,\sigma^{2k}h^{k}+\frac{C_{+}+C_{-}}{2k-Y}\,h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}

(91)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}\right)+O\big(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y/2}\big)+{O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{2k-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

Proof.

By Lemmas
15
,
17
, and
19
, we can compute

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\widehat{X}_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|X_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)=\mathbb{E}\Big(\big(\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}\big)^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}\left(\sigma^{2k}W_{h}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(J_{h}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{(2k)!}{(2j)!\,(2k-2j)!}(\sigma W_{h})^{2j}J_{h}^{2k-2j}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{(2k)!}{(2j+1)!\,(2k-2j-1)!}(\sigma W_{h})^{2j+1}J_{h}^{2k-2j-1}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=(2k-1)!!\,\sigma^{2k}h^{k}+O\big(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}\big)+o\left(h^{k+\frac{1}{Y}}\right)+{O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{2k-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\frac{C_{+}\!+\!C_{-}}{2k-Y}h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}+O\big(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}\big)+O\big(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y/2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}\right)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}\right)+O\left(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y/2-1}\right).

The result then follows.
∎

We are now ready to show that the asymptotic results above hold without the conditions (iii)-(vi) of Assumption
3
.

Lemma 7
.

The asymptotic expansions in Eqs. (
75
) and (
91
) hold true for a Lévy process
J
having a Lévy density of the form (
7
) with
q
satisfying only conditions (i)-(ii) of Assumption
1
.

Proof.

Let us recall the processes
J^{\infty}
and
J^{0}_{t}
introduced in (
101
) and (
102
).
Write

\displaystyle\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle=\sigma W_{t}+J^{\infty}_{t}.

(92)

Note that
J^{\infty}_{t}
satisfies all the conditions of Assumptions
1
and
3
and, therefore,
\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{t}
enjoys the expansions (
75
) and (
91
). We also have that
J^{0}_{t}
is of finite jump activity. Next, writing
V=J^{0}=\widehat{X}-\widehat{X}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{X}_{h}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}-(\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h})^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big|

\displaystyle=\big|V_{h}^{2}+2V_{h}\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h}\big|{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon,~|\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\widehat{X}_{h}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon,~|\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h})^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|>\varepsilon,|\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle=:T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}.

(93)

Since
V
has finite jump activity, for any
p>0
we have

\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left(\left|\frac{V_{h}}{\varepsilon}\right|^{p}{\bf 1}_{\{|V_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}(|V_{h}|>0)\leq Kh.

For
T_{1}
, since
{\bf 1}_{\{|x+v|\leq\varepsilon,|x|\leq\varepsilon\}}\leq{}{\bf 1}_{\{|v|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq K\Big(\mathbb{E}V_{h}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|V_{h}|\leq{2}\varepsilon\}}+\varepsilon\mathbb{E}|V_{h}|{\bf 1}_{\{|V_{h}|\leq{2}\varepsilon\}}\Big)\leq Kh\varepsilon^{2}.

For
T_{2}
, note
{\bf 1}_{\{|x+v|\leq\varepsilon,|x|>\varepsilon\}}\leq{\bf 1}_{\{|v|>0\}}
. So, writing
N^{0}
for the jump measure of
V
, we have
\mathbb{P}(|V_{h}|>0)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(N^{0}([0,h],\mathbb{R})>0\big)\leq Kh
, giving

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{P}(|V_{h}|>0)\leq K\varepsilon^{2}h.

For
T_{3}
, since
{\bf 1}_{\{|x+v|>\varepsilon,|x|\leq\varepsilon\}}\leq{\bf 1}_{\{|v|>0\}}
, we again have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}T_{3}

\displaystyle\leq K\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{P}(|V_{h}|>0)\leq K\varepsilon^{2}h.

Therefore, we obtain

\big|\mathbb{E}\widehat{X}_{h}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}-\mathbb{E}(\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h})^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big|=O(h\varepsilon^{2}),

(94)

which together with the fact that
\widehat{X}^{\prime}
satisfies the asymptotic (
75
), implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\widehat{X}_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}h+\widetilde{A}(\varepsilon,h)h

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-2}\right)+O\big(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-\frac{Y}{2}}\big)+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y}\right)

(95)

\displaystyle\quad+{O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}})}+O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+{O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

We can similarly prove (
91
). Concretely, applying Corollary 2.1.9 in
Jacod and Protter 2011
and Proposition
5
, we obtain

\big|\mathbb{E}\widehat{X}_{h}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}-\mathbb{E}(\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h})^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}^{\prime}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big|=O(h\varepsilon^{2k}),

(96)

which together with the fact that
\widehat{X}^{\prime}
satisfies (
91
), implies the same for
\mathbb{E}\big(\widehat{X}_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\widehat{X}_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big)
.
∎

Appendix D
Bounds for the discretization error of integrals

One of the key tools behind our results is to discretize the integrals involved in the increment
\Delta_{i}^{n}X
. Concretely, we approximate
\Delta_{i}^{n}X
with

\displaystyle x_{i}:=b_{t_{i-1}}h+\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W+\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J=:x_{i,1}+x_{i,2}+x_{i,3}.

(97)

In this section, we aim to provide bounds for the corresponding discretization errors:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{i}:=\Delta_{i}^{n}X-x_{i},\quad\mathcal{E}_{i,1}=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}b_{s}ds-{b}_{t_{i-1}}h,

(98)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{i,2}=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\sigma_{s}dW_{s}-\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}W,\qquad\mathcal{E}_{i,3}=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\chi_{s}dJ_{s}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}J.

Lemma 8
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
and let
\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}
,
\ell=1,2,3
be defined as in (
98
). Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\left|\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}\right|^{p}\right]\leq C\begin{cases}h^{p}&\ell=1,~p>0,\\
h^{\frac{(2\wedge p)+p}{2}}&\ell=2,~p>0,\\
h^{1+\frac{p}{2}}&\ell=3,~p\in[1,\infty)\cap(Y,\infty).\end{cases}

(99)

Proof.

Recall, by virtue of a localization argument, we can assume that the processes
b,\chi
are bounded (by a nonrandom constant).
For
\mathcal{E}_{i,1}
,
\mathbb{E}_{i-1}|\mathcal{E}_{i,1}|^{r}\leq K(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i-1}}ds)^{r}\leq Kh^{r}
.
For
\mathcal{E}_{i,2}
, using the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality and Jensen’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}|\mathcal{E}_{i,2}|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq K\begin{cases}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}|\sigma_{s}-\sigma_{t_{i-1}}|^{2}ds\Big)^{r/2}&0<r<2\\
h^{\frac{r}{2}-1}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}|\sigma_{s}-\sigma_{t_{i-1}}|^{r}ds&r\geq 2.\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq K\begin{cases}h^{r}&0<r<2\\
h^{1+\frac{r}{2}}&r\geq 2.\end{cases}

Turning now to
\mathcal{E}_{i,3}
, let
\delta(s,z)=(\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}})z
, so that we may write
\mathcal{E}_{i,3}=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\delta(s,z)(N(ds,dz)-\nu(dz)ds)
. First observe, for any
r>Y,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\delta(s,z)|^{r}\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\leq K\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}|\chi_{s}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{r}ds\int_{\mathbb{R}}|z|^{r}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{1+\frac{r}{2}}.

(100)

Thus, for the case
p\in(Y,2]\cap[1,2]
, by Lemma 2.1.5 in
Jacod and Protter 2011

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|\mathcal{E}_{i,3}|^{p})

\displaystyle\leq K\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big(\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\delta(s,z)|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{1+\frac{p}{2}}.

For the case
p>2
, again by Lemma 2.1.5 in
Jacod and Protter 2011
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|\mathcal{E}_{i,3}|^{p})

\displaystyle\leq K\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\delta(s,z)|^{p}\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+h^{p/2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{h}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\delta(s,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\bigg)^{p/2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K\bigg(h^{1+\frac{p}{2}}+h^{p/2-1}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\delta(s,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)\bigg)^{p/2}ds\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{1+\frac{p}{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

For the following lemma, we need to laying out some additional notation related to the process
J
.
Recall that
N
denotes the Poisson jump measure of
J
. Let
\bar{N}
be its compensated measure under
\mathbb{P}
. Observe that due to condition (ii) in Assumption
1
, there exists
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
such that
q(x)>0
for all
|x|\leq{}\delta_{0}
. Next, for
p<Y\wedge 1
, let
\breve{J}
be a pure-jump Lévy process independent of
J
with triplet
(0,0,\breve{\nu})
, where
\breve{\nu}(dx)={e^{-|x|^{p}}}(C_{+}{\bf 1}_{(0,\infty)}(x)+C_{-}{\bf 1}_{(-\infty,0)}(x)){\bf 1}_{|x|>\delta_{0}}\,|x|^{-1-Y}dx
and define the Lévy process

J^{\infty}_{t}=\Big(b+\int_{\delta_{0}<|x|\leq 1}x\nu(dx)\Big)t+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{|x|\leq{}\delta_{0}}x\bar{N}(ds,dx)+\breve{J}_{t}.

(101)

In other words,
J^{\infty}
has Lévy measure
\nu(dx){\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq\delta_{0}\}}+\breve{\nu}(dx){\bf 1}_{\{|x|>\delta_{0}\}}
, and, in particular,
J^{\infty}_{t}
satisfies all the conditions of Assumptions
3
. Next, we write

J^{0}_{t}:=J_{t}-J^{\infty}_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{|x|>{}\delta_{0}}x{N}(ds,dx)-\breve{J}_{t},

(102)

and observe
J^{0}
has finite jump activity.

The next lemma gives, for an appropriate
\delta_{n}\to 0
, an upper bound for the quantity
\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty})^{p}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|\leq\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta_{n}\}}]
.

Lemma 9
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
, and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
with
h^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\ll\varepsilon
for some
s>0
. Consider any sequence
\delta_{n}\to 0
satisfying
\delta_{n}\geq h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{Y/2}
. Then, for any
p\geq 0
, with
\mathcal{E}_{i}
as in (
98
),

\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty})^{p}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{\infty}|\leq\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta_{n}\}}]=O(h^{2}\varepsilon^{{p}-2Y}).

(103)

Proof.

Recall that
J^{\infty}
satisfies both Assumptions
1
and
3
. For simplicity in this proof we write
J
in place of
J^{\infty}
, and with slight abuse of notation denote its jump and Lévy measure by
N(ds,dz)
and
\nu(dz)
, respectively.
Using the relation
{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\delta_{n}\}}\leq{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i,1}|>\delta_{n}/3\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i,2}|>\delta_{n}/3\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i,3}|>\delta_{n}/3\}}
, we may consider each
\mathcal{E}_{i,\ell}

\ell=1,2,3
separately. For
\mathcal{E}_{i,1}
, note
\{|\mathcal{E}_{i,1}|>\delta_{n}/3\}=\emptyset
for large enough
n
, since
\delta_{n}\gg h^{1/2}\varepsilon\gg h
, and
|\mathcal{E}_{i,1}|\leq Kh
almost surely. For
\mathcal{E}_{2,i}
, clearly
\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{E}_{i,2}|^{2}\leq Kh^{2}
which using Lemma
17
implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}J)^{p}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i,2}|>\delta_{n}\}}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}J)^{p}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}]\delta_{n}^{-2}\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{E}_{i,2}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{3}\varepsilon^{p-Y}\delta_{n}^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2}\varepsilon^{p-2Y}.

So, it remains to consider
\mathcal{E}_{i,3}
. Choose an
s_{0}>0
small enough so that

h^{1/2}\varepsilon\ll h^{s_{0}}\delta_{n}=:u_{n}.

(104)

For simplicity below we write
u=u_{n}
. Letting
\delta(s,z)=(\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}})z
, and
\bar{N}(ds,dz)=N(ds,dz)-\nu(dz)ds
, define

N(u)=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{|\delta(s,z)|>u\}}}N(ds,dz)

M(u)=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{|\delta(s,z)|\leq u\}}}\delta(s,z){\bar{N}(ds,dz)}

B(u)=-\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{|\delta(s,z)|>u\}}}\delta(s,z)\nu(dz)ds

Since
N(u)=0
implies
\mathcal{E}_{i,3}=M(u)+B(u)
, we have the following:

\displaystyle(\Delta_{i}^{n}J)^{p}

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i,3}|>\delta_{n}\}}

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{i}^{n}J)^{p}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i,3}|>\delta_{n}\}}\big({\bf 1}_{\{N(u)>0\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{N(u)=0\}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{p}({\bf 1}_{\{N(u)>0,|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|M(u)|>\delta_{n}/2\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|B(u)|>\delta_{n}/2\}})

\displaystyle=:T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}.

(105)

Now, for the first term in (
105
), we split up
\Delta_{i}^{n}J
based on jumps of size
2\varepsilon
. For simplicity write
w=2\varepsilon
and consider the decomposition

\Delta_{i}^{n}J=L(w)+M^{\prime}(w)+B^{\prime}(w),

where

L(w)=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{|z|>w\}}}zN(ds,dz),

M^{\prime}(w)=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{|z|\leq w\}}}z{\bar{N}(ds,dz)}\quad B^{\prime}(w)=-\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{|z|>w\}}}z\nu(dz)ds,

Further, let

L_{+}(w)=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{z>w\}}}zN(ds,dz)\quad L_{-}(w)=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{z<-w\}}}zN(ds,dz),

so that
L(w)=L_{+}(w)+L_{-}(w)
. Finally, analogous to
N(u)
, let

N^{\prime}(w)=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{{\{|z|>w\}}}N(ds,dz)=\mu\big(\{z:|z|>w\},(t_{i-1},t_{i}]\big),

and similarly let

N^{\prime}_{+}(w)=\mu\big(\{z:z>w\},(t_{i-1},t_{i}]\big)\quad N^{\prime}_{-}(w)=\mu\big(\{z:z<-w\},(t_{i-1},t_{i}]\big),

i.e.,
N^{\prime}(w)=N^{\prime}_{+}(w)+N^{\prime}_{+}(w)
. Returning to the first term in (
105
), first observe

|\delta(s,z)|>u\iff|z|>|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u.

We now split the set
\{|z|>|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u\}
and consider the cases and
w<|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u
and
w\geq|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u
separately. For the case when
w<|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u
, note

w<|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u\implies N(u)\leq N^{\prime}(w),

since
\{|z|>w\}\supseteq\{|z|\geq|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u\}
. Using the decomposition
\{N^{\prime}(w)>0\}=\{N_{+}^{\prime}(w)>0,N_{-}^{\prime}(w)=0\}\cup\{N_{+}^{\prime}(w)=0,N_{-}^{\prime}(w)>0\}\cup\{N_{+}^{\prime}(w)N_{-}^{\prime}(w)>0\}
, we therefore have

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{w<|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u\}}{\bf 1}_{\{N(u)>0,|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle\leq{\bf 1}_{\{N^{\prime}(w)>0,|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle={\bf 1}_{\{N_{+}^{\prime}(w)>0,N_{-}^{\prime}(w)=0,|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{N_{+}^{\prime}(w)=0,N_{-}^{\prime}(w)>0,|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+{\bf 1}_{\{N_{+}^{\prime}(w)N_{-}^{\prime}(w)>0,|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle=:T_{1,1}+T_{1,2}+T_{1,3}.

(106)

For
T_{1,1},
note the event
\{N_{+}^{\prime}(w)>0,N_{-}^{\prime}(w)=0\}=\{N_{+}^{\prime}(w)\geq 1,N_{-}^{\prime}(w)=0\}
implies

\{L_{+}(w)\geq w,|L(w)|=L_{+}(w)\}
. Also, using that
|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\geq|L(w)|-|M^{\prime}(w)|-|B^{\prime}(w)|
, we have

\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}\subseteq\{|L(w)|-|M^{\prime}(w)|-|B^{\prime}(w)|\leq\varepsilon\}=\{|M^{\prime}(w)|\geq|L(w)|-\varepsilon-|B^{\prime}(w)|\}
. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}T_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(L_{+}(w)\geq w,|L(w)|=L_{+}(w),~|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(L_{+}(w)\geq w,|L(w)|=L_{+}(w),~|M^{\prime}(w)|\geq|L(w)|-\varepsilon-|B^{\prime}(w)|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(L_{+}(w)\geq w,|M^{\prime}(w)|\geq w-\varepsilon-|B^{\prime}(w)|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(L_{+}(w)\geq w\Big)\mathbb{P}\Big(|M^{\prime}(w)|\geq w-\varepsilon-|B^{\prime}(w)|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq K(hw^{-Y})\cdot hw^{2-Y}(w-\varepsilon-|B^{\prime}(w)|)^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq K(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{2},

where we used independence of
L_{+}(w)
and
M^{\prime}(w)
, the inequalities
\mathbb{E}L_{+}(w)\leq Khw^{1-Y}
,
|B^{\prime}(w)|\leq Kh\varepsilon^{1-Y}\ll\varepsilon
, and also that
w=2\varepsilon
. Analogous arguments show that
\mathbb{E}T_{1,2}\leq Kh^{2}\varepsilon^{-2Y}
. For
T_{1,3}
, simply note

\mathbb{E}T_{1,3}\leq\mathbb{P}(N_{+}^{\prime}(w)\geq 1,N_{-}^{\prime}(w)\geq 1)\leq K(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{2}.

Thus, turning back to the first term in (
105
), we obtain from (
106
),

\mathbb{E}\big(T_{1}{\bf 1}_{\{w<|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u\}}\big)\leq\varepsilon^{p}\mathbb{E}(T_{1,1}+T_{1,2}+T_{1,3})\leq h^{2}\varepsilon^{p-2Y}.

(107)

Now we consider the case
\{w\geq|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u\}=\{|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|>u/w\}=\{|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|>u\varepsilon^{-1}/2\}
. Based on (
104
), we may write
u\varepsilon^{-1}=h^{1/2}\phi_{n}
where
\phi_{n}\gg h^{-s_{1}}
for some small
s_{1}>0
. Thus, using Markov’s inequality, for every
r>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(T_{1}{\bf 1}_{\{w\geq|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{-1}u\}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{p}\mathbb{E}\big({\bf 1}_{\{|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|>h^{1/2}\phi_{n}/2\}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq K\varepsilon^{p}(h^{-1/2}\phi_{n}^{-1})^{r}\mathbb{E}|\chi_{s^{-}}-\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq K\varepsilon^{p}\phi_{n}^{-r}=o(h^{2}\varepsilon^{-2Y}),

(108)

by taking
r
large enough.
Now, turning to
T_{2}
in (
105
), By Lemma 2.1.5 in
Jacod and Protter 2011
, with
r\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|M(u)|^{r})

\displaystyle\leq K\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big(\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{\{|\delta(s,z)|<u\}}|\delta(s,z)|^{r}\nu(dz)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+h^{{r}/2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big(\frac{1}{h}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{\{|\delta(s,z)|<u\}}|\delta(s,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\Big)^{{r}/2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K\bigg(u^{{r}-Y}h^{1+Y/2}+{h^{{r}/2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big[\frac{1}{h}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\Big(\int_{\{|\delta(s,z)|<u\}}|\delta(s,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)\Big)^{{r}/2}ds\Big]}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K\big(u^{{r}-Y}h^{1+Y/2}+h^{r/2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq Ku^{{r}-Y}h^{1+Y/2},

where the last is a consequence of the lower bound in assumption (
104
) and that
\varepsilon\gg h^{1/2}
. Thus, taking
r
large enough, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}T_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{p}\mathbb{P}\big(|M(u)|>\delta_{n}/2\big)\leq K\varepsilon^{p}\delta_{n}^{-{r}}\mathbb{E}|M(u)|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{p}\delta_{n}^{-{r}}u^{{r}-Y}h^{1+Y/2}=o(h^{2}\varepsilon^{-2Y}).

(109)

Turning to
T_{3}
, since
\chi
is bounded we have
|B(u)|\leq Khu^{1-Y}\ll Kh^{3/2}\varepsilon^{1-Y}\ll\delta_{n}
, implying
T_{3}=0
for all large
n
.
Thus, based on (
107
), (
108
), (
109
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{i}^{n}J)^{p}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i,3}|>\delta_{n}\}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}T_{1}+\mathbb{E}T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\big(h^{2}\varepsilon^{p-2Y}+o(h^{2}\varepsilon^{p-2Y})\big).

(110)

This establishes (
103
).
∎

Our last result of this section gives an estimate for the discretization error of approximating Riemann integrals by Riemann sums in the case where the integrand is an Itô semimartingale.

Lemma 10
.

As
h\to 0
,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma^{2}_{t_{i-1}}h-\int_{0}^{1}\sigma_{s}^{2}ds=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

and, for all
0<Y<2
, with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon\ll 1
,

\varepsilon^{2-Y}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{Y}h-\varepsilon^{2-Y}\int_{0}^{1}|\chi_{s}|^{Y}ds=O_{P}(\varepsilon^{2-Y}h^{\frac{Y\wedge 1}{2}})

\text{and}\quad h\varepsilon^{-Y}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\chi_{t_{i-1}}|^{Y}\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}h-h\varepsilon^{-Y}\int_{0}^{1}|\chi_{s}|^{Y}\sigma_{s}^{2}ds=o_{P}(\varepsilon^{2-Y}h^{\frac{Y\wedge 1}{2}}).

Proof.

Consider

A_{n}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(\sigma_{t_{i-1}},\chi_{t_{i}-1})h-\int_{0}^{1}f_{n}(\sigma_{s},\chi_{s})ds

for the cases
f_{n}(v,x)=v^{2}
,
f_{n}(v,x)=\varepsilon^{2-Y}|x|^{Y}
, and
f_{n}(v,x)=h\varepsilon^{-Y}v^{2}|x|^{Y}
. The case for
f_{n}(v,x)=v^{2}
, establishing
A_{n}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
is standard (e.g. one can argue as in (5.3.24) in
Jacod and Protter 2011
) and hence we omit its proof. For the case
f_{n}(v,x)=h\varepsilon^{-Y}v^{2}|x|^{Y}
,

|f_{n}(\sigma_{t},\chi_{t})-f_{n}(\sigma_{s},\chi_{s})|\leq K\varepsilon^{2-Y}|\chi_{t}-\chi_{s}|^{Y\wedge 1}

which implies
\mathbb{E}|A_{n}|\leq\varepsilon^{2-Y}h^{\frac{Y\wedge 1}{2}}.
For the remaining case
f_{n}(v,x)=h\varepsilon^{-Y}v^{2}|x|^{Y}
, since
h\ll\varepsilon^{2}
, we have the same bound:
|f_{n}(\sigma_{t},\chi_{t})-f_{n}(\sigma_{s},\chi_{s})|\leq Kh\varepsilon^{-Y}|\chi_{t}-\chi_{s}|^{Y\wedge 1}\ll\varepsilon^{2-Y}|\chi_{t}-\chi_{s}|^{Y\wedge 1}
, which again gives
\mathbb{E}|A_{n}|=o(\varepsilon^{2-Y}h^{\frac{Y\wedge 1}{2}})
.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs for the stable-in-law CLT results in
Boniece et al. 2023

In this section, we verify the technical condition (2.2.40) in
Jacod and Protter 2011
to conclude the stable convergence of our estimators.

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that
h^{\beta}\ll\varepsilon\ll h^{\frac{1}{4-Y}}
, for some
\frac{1}{4-Y}<\beta<\frac{1}{2}
.
Define

\xi_{i}^{n}:=\sqrt{n}\left(\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon\}}-\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\big(\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big)\right).

Then, as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\xi_{i}^{n}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0,

(111)

when
M=W
or
M
is a square-integrable martingale orthogonal to
W
.

Proof.

Recall the notation (
97
)-(
98
).
We need to show that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon\}}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0,

We treat each term in the expansion
(x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i})^{2}=x_{i}^{2}+2x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}
separately.
For the second and third terms, we use Cauchy’s inequality. Indeed, for
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}
,

\displaystyle n\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon\}}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\right|^{2}
