\displaystyle\leq n\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{4}]\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq{}n\sum_{i=1}^{n}O_{P}(h^{3})O_{P}(1)\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0,

since
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[(M_{1}-M_{0})^{2}]<\infty
. For the term
2x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, when
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon
, since
h^{\frac{1}{2}}/\varepsilon\ll h^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}
, we can use Markov’s inequality to conclude
\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon)\leq Kh^{1+(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)r}
for all
r>Y
(c.f. (
99
)) and can apply Hölder’s inequality to show that

\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}|{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon\}}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]=o_{P}(1).

When
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon
, note
|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|=|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon
implies that
|x_{i}|\leq{}2\varepsilon
. Next,

\displaystyle n\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i}x_{i}|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|\right]\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}]\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq{}n\sum_{i=1}^{n}O_{P}(h^{\frac{3}{2}})O_{P}(h)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0,

where in the last inequality we apply Proposition

5
.
All that remains is to show

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0.

(112)

Now, when
x_{i}
is replaced with
b_{t_{i-1}}h
in (
112
), we proceed as follows:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}b^{2}_{t_{i-1}}h^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\right|\leq Ch^{2}\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(113)

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{2}n\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\to{}0,

where we used

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq{}n^{1/2}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]\big)^{1/2}
.
We also have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Big|

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{t_{i-1}}h\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\hat{x}_{i}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Ch\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\hat{x}_{i}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ch\sqrt{n}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\hat{x}_{i}^{2}\right]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\to}}{}0.

So, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\hat{x}_{i}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0,

(114)

where recall that
\hat{x}_{i}=\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W+\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J
.
We first prove it when
M=W
.
When we replace
\hat{x}_{i}
with
\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W
in (
114
), we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Big|

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Delta_{i}^{n}M\right]\Big|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{3}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon\}}\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{3}|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\right]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{6}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon}\to{}0.

When
\hat{x}_{i}
is replaced with
\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J
in (
114
), from boundedness of
\chi
, we need only to show that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\right]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0.

(115)

Note that when
|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|>{}\varepsilon
or
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon
, we can use Hölder’s inequality, and that
\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon)
and
\mathbb{P}(|\Delta^{n}_{i}W|>\varepsilon)
decay faster than any power of
h
(a consequence of
h^{\frac{1}{2}}/\varepsilon\ll h^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}
) to obtain

\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|({\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|>{}\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon\}})=o_{P}(1).

So, when
|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\leq{}\varepsilon
and
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon
, note
|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon
implies that
|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}C\varepsilon
, for some positive constant
C
that we assume for simplicity is
1
. Then, for (
115
), it suffices to establish that
\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\right]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0
. By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
17
, for any
p,q>1
such that
1/p+1/q=1
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\right]

(116)

\displaystyle\leq{}\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2p}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{q}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(h\varepsilon^{2p-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{}Cn^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{p}};

so, making
p
close to
1
(and
q
large), we get the convergence to
0
, establishing (
114
) when
M=W
.

Now, we show (
114
) for any bounded square integrable
M
that is orthogonal to
W
. We first consider the case when
\hat{x}_{i}
is replaced with
\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J
. As before, when
|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|>{}\varepsilon
or
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon
we can use Hölder’s inequality and the fast decay of
\mathbb{P}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|>\varepsilon)
and
\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon)
to conclude

\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 3\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|({\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|>{}\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon\}})]=o_{P}(1).

When
|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\leq{}\varepsilon
and
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon
, then
|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon
implies
|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}3\varepsilon
and we proceed as follows:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 3\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|^{4}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 3\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|^{4}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 3\varepsilon\}}\right]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{n}(nh\varepsilon^{4-Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}O_{P}(1)=C(h^{-1}\varepsilon^{4-Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}O_{P}(1)=o_{P}(1),

since, by assumption,
\varepsilon\ll h^{1/(4-Y)}
. The cross-product when expanding
\hat{x}_{i}^{2}
can be analyzed as follows (again, assuming that
|\Delta_{i}J|\leq{}3\varepsilon
):

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J||\Delta_{i}^{n}W|{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 3\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq{}\sqrt{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 3\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 3\varepsilon\}}\right]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(nh\varepsilon^{2-Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}O_{P}(1)=o_{P}(1).

Finally, we consider the case when
\hat{x}_{i}
is replaced with
\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W
. Note that
\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{2}=2\int_{t_{i-1}}(W_{s}-W_{t_{i-1}})dW_{s}+h
and, since
M
is orthogonal to
W
,
\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{2}-h)\Delta_{i}^{n}M\right]=0
, which in turn implies that
\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{2}\Delta_{i}^{n}M\right]=0
. Thus, for any
p,q>0
such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Delta_{i}^{n}M\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad=\sqrt{n}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon\}}\Delta_{i}^{n}M\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C\sqrt{n}}{\varepsilon}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{2}|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}||\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C\sqrt{n}}{\varepsilon}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W)|^{2p}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{q}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C\sqrt{n}}{\varepsilon}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{2p}\right]^{\frac{2}{p}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{q}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C\sqrt{n}}{\varepsilon}\left(nh^{2}h^{\frac{2}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}O_{P}(1)\leq C\frac{h^{\frac{1}{q}}}{\varepsilon}O_{P}(1)\ll Ch^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta-s}\varepsilon^{-1}=o_{P}(1),

for all small
s>0
by taking
q
close enough to
2
and
p
large enough. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 12
.

Suppose that
{h^{\beta}}\ll\varepsilon\ll h^{\frac{1}{4-Y}}
with
\frac{1}{4-Y}<\beta<\frac{1}{2}\wedge\frac{2}{3Y}
, and let

\tilde{\xi}_{i}^{n}:=\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\left(\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\zeta\varepsilon\}}-\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\big(\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\zeta\varepsilon\}}\big)\right).

Then, as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\tilde{\xi}_{i}^{n}({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0,

(117)

when
M=W
or
M
is a square-integrable martingale orthogonal to
W
.

Proof.

Recall the notation (
97
)-(
98
).
Fix
f(x)={\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|x|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}
. We need to prove that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2}f\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]=o_{P}(1).

(118)

Similar to the proof of Lemma 7 in
Aït-Sahalia and Jacod 2009
, there exists a
C^{2}
smooth approximation
f_{n}
of
f
such that, for any
\eta>0
,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon(1+\frac{2}{3}\varepsilon^{\eta})<|x|<\zeta\varepsilon(1-\frac{2}{3}\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}\leq{}f_{n}(x)\leq{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon(1+\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{\eta})<|x|<\zeta\varepsilon(1-\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{\eta})\}},\\
|f_{n}^{\prime}(x)|\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{1+\eta}},\quad|f_{n}^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq{}\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2+2\eta}}.\end{array}\right.

To show (
118
), first observe it suffices to prove the statement replacing
f
with
f_{n}
. To see this, we need to show that

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{2}(f-f_{n})(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\to 0,

which in turn follows from

\displaystyle A_{n,1}

\displaystyle:=\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq{}|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|\right]=o_{P}(1),

\displaystyle A_{n,2}

\displaystyle:=\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})\leq|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq\zeta\varepsilon\}}|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|\right]=o_{P}(1).

Applying Cauchy’s inequality and (
48
),

\displaystyle|A_{n,1}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}X^{4}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq{}|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{}C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\left(h\varepsilon^{4-Y}\varepsilon^{\eta}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=O_{P}(\varepsilon^{\frac{\eta}{2}}),

since, by Cauchy’s inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq{}n^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=O_{P}(h_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

(121)

because
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[(M_{1}-M_{0})^{2}]<\infty
. The same argument shows
A_{n,2}=o_{P}(1)
. Thus it suffices to show

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2}f_{n}\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]=o_{P}(1).

(122)

We recall the notation (
97
)-(
98
). We consider separately the three terms in the expansion
(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)^{2}=(x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i})^{2}=x_{i}^{2}+2x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}
.
For the second and third terms, we use Cauchy’s inequality. Indeed, by Lemma
8
,

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}f_{n}\big(\Delta_{i}^{n}X\big)|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|\right]\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\leq{}C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}=o_{P}(1),

since recall that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]^{1/2}=O_{P}(h^{-1/2})
and

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h\ll 1\;\Longleftrightarrow\;h^{\frac{2}{4-Y}}\ll\varepsilon\;\Longleftarrow\;h^{1/2}\ll\varepsilon\;\text{ because }\;\frac{2}{4-Y}>\frac{1}{2}.

For the term
x_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, note that when
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon
, we can use the bound

\mathbb{P}_{i-1}(|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[|\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{r}]}{\varepsilon^{r}}\leq Kh^{1+r(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)}.

(which is valid for all large
r
due to (
99
) and
\varepsilon\gg h^{\beta}
) and Hölder’s inequality to conclude

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i}x_{i}|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq C\varepsilon\}}|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon\}}\right]=o_{P}(1).

When
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\varepsilon
, observe
|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon
implies that
|x_{i}|=|\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq(1+\zeta)\varepsilon=:C\varepsilon
. Next, by (
91
), (
99
), and (
121
),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}

\displaystyle\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i}x_{i}|{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq C\varepsilon\}}|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\mathcal{E}_{i}^{4}]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[x_{i}^{4}{\bf 1}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq C\varepsilon\}}]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{}C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{3}{4}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}=o_{P}(1),

since
h^{\beta}\ll\varepsilon
implies that
\varepsilon^{(Y-4)/2}h^{3/4}\ll h^{3/4-\beta(4-Y)/2}\ll 1
.

Therefore, we only need to show that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[x_{i}^{2}f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]=o_{P}(1).

(123)

When
x_{i}
is replaced with
b_{t_{i-1}}h
in (
123
), we have:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}b^{2}_{t_{i-1}}h^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\Big|

(124)

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=O_{P}(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}})=o_{P}(1),

which follows from the assumption
h^{\beta}\ll\varepsilon
, since
\frac{3}{4-Y}>\frac{1}{2}>\beta
. We also have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{t_{i-1}}h

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\hat{x}_{i}f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Ch\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\hat{x}_{i}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ch\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|{\Delta_{i}^{n}M}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=O_{P}(h\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}),

whose convergence to
0
follows from
h^{\beta}\ll\varepsilon
, since
\frac{2}{4-Y}>\frac{1}{2}>\beta
. So, it suffices to show

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\hat{x}_{i}^{2}f_{n}(\Delta_{i}X)({\Delta_{i}^{n}M})\right]=o_{P}(1),

(125)

where recall that
\hat{x}_{i}=\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W+\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J
.
We first prove it when
M=W
.
When we replace
\hat{x}_{i}
with
\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W
in (
114
), for any
q,p>0
such that
1/q+1/p=1
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{2}f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)\Delta_{i}^{n}M\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{3}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|>\varepsilon\}}\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{3p}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|>\varepsilon\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}nh^{\frac{3}{2}}(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}},

where we applied Lemma
6
in the third line.
We can make the above bound to converge to
0
by taking
q
close to
1
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}}\ll 1\;\Longleftarrow\;h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{-4-Y}{2}}\ll 1\;\Longleftarrow\;h^{\frac{3}{4+Y}}\ll\varepsilon\;\Longleftarrow\;h^{1/2}\ll\varepsilon

since
\frac{3}{4+Y}>\frac{1}{2}
. When
\hat{x}_{i}
is replaced with
\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J
in (
114
), using boundedness of
\chi
we need only to show that

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\chi_{t_{i-1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\right]=o_{P}(1),

(126)

When
|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|>{}\varepsilon
or
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon
, we can use Hölder’s inequality, and that
\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon)
and
\mathbb{P}(|\Delta^{n}_{i}W|>\varepsilon)
both decay faster than any power of
h
(a consequence of
h^{\frac{1}{2}}/\varepsilon\ll h^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}
) to conclude that

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|({\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|>{}\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>{}\varepsilon\}})]=o_{P}(1).

So, together
|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\leq{}\varepsilon
,
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq{}\varepsilon
, and
|\Delta_{i}X|=|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\leq\zeta\varepsilon
imply that
|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}C\varepsilon
, for some positive constant
C
that, for simplicity, we assume is
2
. Then, for (
126
) to hold, it suffices that

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\right]\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,0.

To show the latter, by Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
17
, for any
p,q>1
such that
1/p+1/q=1
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}

\displaystyle\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\right]

(127)

\displaystyle\leq{}\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2p}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq 2\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{q}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(h\varepsilon^{2p-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq{}C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{p}};

thus, making
p
very close to
1
(and
q\to\infty
), we get the convergence to
0
since

\varepsilon^{\frac{-Y}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll 1\;\Longleftrightarrow\;h^{\frac{1}{Y}}\ll\varepsilon\;\Longleftarrow\;h^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll\varepsilon.

We now show (
125
) for any bounded square integrable
M
that is orthogonal to
W
. The cross-product when expanding
\hat{x}_{i}^{2}
can be analyzed as follows (again, assuming that
|\Delta_{i}J|\leq{}C\varepsilon
):

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J||\Delta_{i}^{n}W|{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq C\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq{}\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq C\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}(h\varepsilon^{2-Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad=Ch^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1}O_{P}(1)=o_{P}(1),

because
h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1}\ll 1
. Next, we consider the case when
\hat{x}_{i}
is replaced with
\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W
. Since
f_{n}(x)\leq{}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq{}\varepsilon\}}
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle D_{n}:=\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{t_{i-1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}W^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}X|\geq{}\varepsilon\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\right]=o_{P}(1).

As before,
|\Delta_{i}X|=|x_{i}+\mathcal{E}_{i}|\geq{}\varepsilon
implies that
|\Delta_{i}W|>\varepsilon/3
,
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\varepsilon/3
, or
|\Delta_{i}J|>\varepsilon/3
. The first two cases are straightforward. For the last one, we have, for any
p,q>0
such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}
,

\displaystyle D_{n}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[(\Delta_{i}^{n}W)|^{2p}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\geq\varepsilon/3\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}}O_{P}(1).

Using that
\varepsilon\gg h^{\beta}
, the last expression above is such that

\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{\frac{1}{q}}\ll h^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{q}-\beta\left(2-\frac{Y}{2}+\frac{Y}{q}\right)}

As
q\downarrow 2
, the exponent
{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{q}-\beta\left(2-\frac{Y}{2}+\frac{Y}{q}\right)}\to 1-2\beta>0
, hence for
q>2
close enough to 2 we have
D_{n}=o_{P}(1)
.

Finally, we consider the case when
\hat{x}_{i}
is replaced with
\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J
in (
125
). That is, we need to show that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)(\Delta_{i}^{n}M)\right]=o_{P}(1).

(128)

As shown in
F
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}|f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)-f_{n}(\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J)||\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\right]=o_{P}(1).

(129)

Therefore, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}f_{n}(\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J)(\Delta_{i}^{n}M)\right]=o_{P}(1).

(130)

For simplicity in the remaining part of the argument we assume
\chi\equiv 1
; the general case can be obtained by a near-identical argument. Let us first introduce some notation. By redefining the process
b
if necessary, we may assume that the Lévy-Itô decomposition of
J
takes the form

J_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int x{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq{}1\}}\bar{N}(ds,dx)+\int_{0}^{t}\int x{\bf 1}_{\{|x|>{}1\}}N(ds,dx),

where
\bar{N}(ds,dx)=N(ds,dx)-\nu(dx)ds
is the compensated jump measure of
J
. We also recall
M
admits the decomposition

M_{t}=M^{\prime}_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}\int\delta(s,x)\bar{N}(ds,dx),

where
M^{\prime}
is a martingale orthogonal to both
W
and
N
, and
\delta
is bounded such that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\int_{t}^{u}\int\delta^{2}(s,x)\nu(dx)ds\right|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]\leq{}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(M_{u}-M_{t})^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]<\infty
(see expression (80) in
Aït-Sahalia and Jacod 2009
for details). For future reference, set
N^{i}(ds,dx):=N(t_{i}+ds,dx)
,
\delta^{i}(s,x):=\delta(t_{i}+s,x)
,
p_{n}(x)=x^{2}f_{n}(x)
, and

v_{n}(y,x)=p_{n}(x+y)-p_{n}(y),\quad w_{n}(y,x)=p_{n}(x+y)-p_{n}(y)-xp_{n}^{\prime}(y){\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq{}1\}}.

By Itô’s formula applied to
Y^{i-1}_{u}:=J_{t_{i-1}+u}-J_{t_{i-1}}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{u})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{u}p_{n}^{\prime}(Y^{i-1}_{s^{-}})dY^{i-1}_{s}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{u}\int(p_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{s^{-}}+x)-p_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{s^{-}})-p_{n}^{\prime}(Y^{i-1}_{s^{-}})x)N^{i-1}(ds,dx)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{|x|\leq{}1}p_{n}^{\prime}(Y^{i-1}_{s^{-}})x\bar{N}^{i-1}(ds,dx)+\int_{0}^{u}\int w_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{s^{-}},x)N^{i-1}(ds,dx).

With the notation
Z^{i-1}_{u}:=M_{t_{i-1}+u}-M_{t_{i-1}}
, Itô’s formula gives:

\displaystyle p_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{u})Z^{i-1}_{u}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{u}p_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{s^{-}})dZ^{i-1}_{s}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{u}Z^{i-1}_{s^{-}}d(p_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{s}))+\sum_{s\leq{}u}\Delta(p_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{s}))\Delta Z^{i-1}_{s}

\displaystyle={\rm Mrtg}+\int_{0}^{u}\int\delta^{i-1}(s,x)v_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{s},x)\nu(dx)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{u}\int w_{n}(Y^{i-1}_{s},x)Z^{i-1}_{s}\nu(dx)ds.

(131)

Fixing
u=h_{n}
in (
131
) and taking expectations, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[p_{n}(\Delta_{i}J)\Delta_{i}^{n}M]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int\delta^{i-1}(s,x)v_{n}(Y_{s},x)\nu(dx)ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int w_{n}(Y_{s},x)Z_{s}\nu(dx)ds\bigg],

(132)

where, for simplicity, we omitted the superscript
i-1
in
Y
and
Z
.
We will analyze each of the two terms in (
132
) separately. We start with the second term. We claim the following bound follows from (
E
):

\displaystyle|w_{n}(y,x)|

\displaystyle\leq{}K{\bf 1}_{|y|\leq\varepsilon\text{ or }|y|\geq{}\zeta\varepsilon}{\bf 1}_{|x|>\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}\varepsilon^{2}

(133)

\displaystyle\quad+K{\bf 1}_{\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\leq|y|\leq\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})}{\bf 1}_{|x|\leq\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}x^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+K{\bf 1}_{\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\leq|y|\leq\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})}{\bf 1}_{|x|\geq\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}(2\varepsilon|x|{\bf 1}_{|x|\leq{}1}+\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+K{\bf 1}_{\varepsilon\leq{}|y|\leq\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})}(\varepsilon^{-2\eta}{\bf 1}_{|x|\leq\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}x^{2}+\varepsilon^{1-\eta}{\bf 1}_{|x|\geq\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}(|x|\wedge 1))

\displaystyle\quad+K{\bf 1}_{\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})\leq{}|y|\leq\zeta\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-2\eta}{\bf 1}_{|x|\leq\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}x^{2}+\varepsilon^{1-\eta}{\bf 1}_{|x|\geq\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}(|x|\wedge 1)).

To see this, first note
|p_{n}(z)|\leq K(\varepsilon\wedge|z|)^{2}
for all
z
. For the first term, observe
|y|\leq\varepsilon
or
|y|>\zeta\varepsilon\implies p_{n}(y)=0
, giving
|w_{n}(y,x)|=p_{n}(x+y)=p_{n}(x+y){\mathbf{1}}_{\{|x|>\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\leq\varepsilon^{2}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|x|>\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}
; for the second term, when both
\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\leq|y|\leq\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})
and
|x|\leq\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}
, then
\varepsilon(1+\frac{2}{3}\varepsilon^{\eta})\leq|x+y|\leq\zeta\varepsilon(1-\frac{2}{3}\varepsilon^{\eta})
, implying
f_{n}(x+y)=1=f_{n}(y)
, so
w_{n}(y,x)=x^{2}
; for the third term, under the same restriction on
y
again we have
f_{n}(y)=1
and
p^{\prime}_{n}(y)=2y
, and using
p_{n}(x+y)\leq K\varepsilon^{2}
gives
|w_{n}(y,x)|\leq K(\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon|x|{\bf 1}_{|x|\leq{}1})
; for the fourth term, when
|x|\leq\varepsilon^{1+\eta}
, a second order Taylor expansion and the restriction
|y|\leq\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})
gives
|w_{n}(y,x)|\leq Kx^{2}\varepsilon^{-2\eta}
and when
|x|>\varepsilon^{1+\eta}
, the mean value theorem gives
|w_{n}(y,x)|\leq 2|xp^{\prime}_{n}(y)|{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}}+p_{n}(y){\bf 1}_{\{|x|>1\}}\leq K\big(|x|\varepsilon^{1-\eta}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}}+\varepsilon^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|>1\}}\big)\leq K\varepsilon^{1-\eta}(1\wedge|x|);
the fifth term is similar to the fourth term.

Therefore, using that
\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|Z_{s}|)\leq{}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|Z_{h_{n}}|)=\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|)
and that
M
is bounded,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big(

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|w_{n}(Y_{s},x)||Z_{s}|\nu(dx)ds\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq K(\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}\vee\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|Z_{s}|)ds

(134)

\displaystyle\quad+K\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}h_{n}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}_{i-1}({\varepsilon\leq{}|Y_{uh_{n}}|\leq\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})})du

\displaystyle\quad+K\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}h_{n}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}_{i-1}({\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})\leq{}|Y_{uh_{n}}|\leq\zeta\varepsilon})du

\displaystyle\leq{}K(\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}\vee\varepsilon)h_{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|)+K\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}h_{n}^{2}{\varepsilon^{-Y}}.

(135)

(In (
134
) the first term following the inequality bounds the first three terms in the corresponding bound (
133
).) To obtain the second bound in (
135
), we used Lemma
17
and the following argument:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon\leq{}|J_{h_{n}u}|\leq\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[J_{h_{n}u}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq{}|J_{h_{n}u}|\leq\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{uh_{n}\varepsilon^{2-Y}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\leq{}Ch_{n}\varepsilon^{-Y}.

(136)

From expression (
132
) and the bound (
135
), we need to show that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}(\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}\vee\varepsilon)h_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|)+\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}h_{n}^{2}\varepsilon^{\eta-Y}h_{n}^{-1}=o_{P}(1).

The first term converges to
0
when
Y>1
since
h_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|)=O_{P}(1)
and
\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}h_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}=\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{2}-Y\eta}h_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll 1
, if
\eta
is small enough; when
Y\leq 1
, the first term is of order
\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-2}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll\varepsilon^{-1}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll 1
. The order of the second term can be written as
\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{2-Y(1+\eta)}h_{n}^{2}\varepsilon^{\eta-Y}h_{n}^{-1}=\varepsilon^{-\frac{3Y}{2}+(1-Y)\eta}h_{n}
which converges to 0 for all small
\eta
, since by assumption
\beta<\frac{2}{3Y}
.

It remains to bound the first term of (
132
). In other words, we need to show that

\displaystyle F_{n}:=\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int\delta^{i-1}(s,x)v_{n}(Y_{s},x)\nu(dx)ds\right]=o_{P}(1).

(137)

To show the above, we separately consider cases where
|Y_{s}|\leq\varepsilon
,
|Y_{s}|>\zeta\varepsilon
,
\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})<|Y_{s}|<\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})
,
\varepsilon\leq{}|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})
and
\zeta\varepsilon\leq{}|Y_{s}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})
.

So, suppose first that
|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon
, so that
f_{n}(Y_{s})=0
,
v_{n}(Y_{s},x)=(Y_{s}+x)^{2}f_{n}(Y_{s}+x)
and, in particular,
|x|\geq{}\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}
(otherwise,
f_{n}(Y_{s}+x)=0
). Starting from the bound
|v_{n}(Y_{s},x)|\leq Y_{s}^{2}+2|xY_{s}|+x^{2}
, we proceed to control each term corresponding to
Y_{s}^{2}
,
2|xY_{s}|
, and
x^{2}
, separately. For
Y_{s}^{2}
, since Lemma
17
implies
\mathbb{E}_{i-1}[Y_{s}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}]\leq Kh\varepsilon^{2k-Y}
for all
0<s<h_{n}
, we have:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|Y_{s}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq{}\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int Y_{s}^{4}{\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq{}\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\left(\varepsilon^{-Y(1+\eta)}h^{2}\varepsilon^{4-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int|\delta(u,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)du\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq Ch\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{2}(1+\eta)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}M)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{2}(1+\eta)}O_{P}(1),

which, due to our assumption
\varepsilon\gg h^{\beta}
, converges to
0
if
\eta
is small enough, because
h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{2}}\ll 1\Longleftarrow h^{\frac{1}{Y}}\ll\varepsilon\Longleftarrow h^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll\varepsilon
. For
2Y_{s}x
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)||Y_{s}x|{\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq{}\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int Y_{s}^{2}x^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq{}\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int|\delta(u,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)du\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq Ch\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}M)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-1}O_{P}(1),

which again is
o_{P}(1)
since,
\varepsilon\gg h^{\frac{1}{2}}
. The proof for the case
x^{2}
is more involved. First note that when
f_{n}(Y_{s}+x)>0
,
|Y_{s}+x|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon
, and together with our assumption
|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon
this gives
|x|\leq{}(1+\zeta)\varepsilon=:C\varepsilon
. Then, for any sequence
\alpha_{n}>0
such that
\alpha_{n}\to{}0
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int_{|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|\leq{}\alpha_{n}}|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|x^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq{}C\varepsilon\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int_{|x|\leq{}C\varepsilon}x^{4}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int_{|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|\leq{}\alpha_{n}}|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\left(h\varepsilon^{4-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{|\delta(u,x)|\leq{}\alpha_{n}}|\delta(u,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\right]

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1),

since

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big[\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int_{|\delta(u,x)|\leq{}\alpha_{n}}|\delta(u,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\Big]\Big]\\

=\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{|\delta(u,x)|\leq{}\alpha_{n}}|\delta(u,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\Big]\to{}0.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int_{|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|>{}\alpha_{n}}|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|x^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq{}C\varepsilon\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\frac{1}{\alpha_{n}}\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int_{|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|>{}\alpha_{n}}|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{Y}{2}}}{\alpha_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int|\delta(u,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\right]=\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{Y}{2}}}{\alpha_{n}}O_{P}(1).

Thus, it suffices to take
\alpha_{n}\to{}0
such that
\varepsilon^{Y/2}/\alpha_{n}\to 0
(e.g.,
\alpha_{n}=\varepsilon^{Y/4}
). This finishes the bound for
F_{n}
for the case when
|Y_{s}|\leq\varepsilon
.

For the case of
|Y_{s}|\geq{}\zeta\varepsilon
, we have
f_{n}(Y_{s})=0
and
v_{n}(Y_{s},x)=(Y_{s}+x)^{2}f_{n}(Y_{s}+x)\leq{}(\zeta\varepsilon)^{2}
. Moreover, whenever
f_{n}(Y_{s}+x)>0
, we have
|Y_{s}+x|\leq\zeta\varepsilon(1-\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{\eta})
, implying
|x|>\zeta\varepsilon^{1+\eta}/3
. Thus,

\displaystyle\qquad\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|(Y_{s}+x)^{2}

(138)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times f_{n}(Y_{s}+x){\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\geq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq{}C\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int{\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\geq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq{}C\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{-Y-{(1+\eta)Y}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\int|\delta(u,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)du\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq Ch\varepsilon^{-{\frac{(1+\eta)Y}{2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}M)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-{\frac{(1+\eta)Y}{2}}}O_{P}(1)=o_{P}(1),

for all small
\eta>0
due to our assumption
\varepsilon\gg h^{\beta}
, which completes the bound for
F_{n}
in the case
|Y_{s}|\geq{}\zeta\varepsilon
.

We now consider the case when
\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})<|Y_{s}|<\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})
. When
|x|\geq{}\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}
, using the bound
|v_{n}(Y_{s},x)|\leq C\varepsilon^{2}
we can follow a similar proof as in (
138
). If
|x|\leq{}\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}
, then
f_{n}(x+Y_{s})=1=f(Y_{s})
, implying
v_{n}(Y_{s},x)=x^{2}+2xY_{s}
; we bound each term separately. For the case of
x^{2}
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|x^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})<|Y_{s}|<\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq{}C\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int_{|x|\leq{}C\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}x^{4}{\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\geq{}\varepsilon\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\left(\varepsilon^{(4-Y)(1+\eta)}h^{2}\varepsilon^{-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[{\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}M)^{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon^{\frac{\eta(4-Y)-Y}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}O_{P}(1)=o_{P}(1),

for all
\eta>0
. For the case of
2|xY_{s}|
, since
|Y_{s}|<\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})
, for some
C
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)||xY_{s}|{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})<|Y_{s}|<\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq{}C\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\bigg[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int_{|x|\leq{}C\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}x^{2}Y_{s}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|Y_{s}|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\nu(dx)ds\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\left(\varepsilon^{(2-Y)(1+\eta)}h^{2}\varepsilon^{2-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[{\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}M)^{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{2}+\frac{\eta(2-Y)}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}O_{P}(1)=o_{P}(1),

again for all
\eta>0
. This completes the bound for
F_{n}
for the case
\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})<|Y_{s}|<\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})
.

The only cases left are when
\varepsilon\leq{}Y_{s}\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})
and
\zeta\varepsilon\leq{}Y_{s}\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})
. We analyze only the first case (the other case follows similarly). If
|x|\geq{}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}
, we can repeat the same argument as in (
138
). Suppose that
|x|\leq{}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}
. Note that
|p_{n}^{\prime}(y)|\leq{}2|yf_{n}(y)|+|y^{2}f_{n}^{\prime}(y)|\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta}
, when
|y|\leq C\varepsilon
and, in particular,
|v_{n}({Y_{s}},x)|\leq{|p^{\prime}_{n}(Y_{s},x)||x|}\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta}|x|
. Thus,
F_{n}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-\eta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)||x|{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq{}|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq{}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-\eta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int|\delta^{i-1}(s,x)|^{2}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\int_{0}^{h_{n}}\!\!\!\!\int_{|x|\leq{}C\varepsilon^{1+\eta}}x^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq{}|Y_{s}|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}\nu(dx)ds\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\varepsilon^{1-\eta}\left(\varepsilon^{(2-Y)(1+\eta)}h^{2}\varepsilon^{\eta-Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[{\mathbb{E}_{i-1}(\Delta_{i}^{n}M)^{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon^{-\frac{Y}{2}+\frac{\eta(1-Y)}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}O_{P}(1),

where we used (
136
). The expression above is
o_{P}(1)
if
\eta
is small enough.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Other Technical Proofs

We follow the same notation and assumptions introduced in Sections
2
and
C
. In particular, unless otherwise stated, throughout this appendix we continue to assume (
60
) in all asymptotic expressions below and assume that the Lévy process
J
satisfies all the conditions (i)-(vi) of Assumptions
1
and
3
. Let us recall the Fourier transform and its inverse:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}g)(z):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(x)e^{-izx}\,dx,\quad\big(\mathcal{F}^{-1}g\big)(x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(z)e^{izx}\,dz.

(139)

We recall the following bounds for the tail probabilities and density of
S_{1}
under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
, which hereafter we denote
p_{S}
:

\displaystyle p_{S}(\pm z)

\displaystyle\leq{K}\,z^{-Y-1},

(140)

\displaystyle\Big|p_{S}(\pm z)-C_{\pm}z^{-Y-1}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{K}\big(z^{-Y-1}\wedge z^{-2Y-1}\big),

(141)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\big(\!\pm\!S_{1}>z\big)

\displaystyle\leq{K}z^{-Y},

(142)

\displaystyle\bigg|\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\big(\!\pm\!S_{1}>z\big)-\frac{C_{\pm}}{Y}z^{-Y}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq{K}z^{-2Y},

(143)

for a constant
{K}<\infty
. The inequalities above can be derived from the asymptotics in Section 14 of
Sato 1999
(see
Figueroa-López et al. 2016
for more details).

The following lemma is used in the proof of Proposition
3
.

Lemma 13
.

Let
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
, fix
\gamma_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, and let
J^{\prime}_{h}=S_{h}+\gamma_{0}h
. Then, as
h\to 0
, with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon\ll 1
,

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left(\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle=C_{\pm}\sigma h^{\frac{3}{2}}(\varepsilon\pm\gamma_{0}h)^{-Y-1}+O\left(h^{\frac{5}{2}}\varepsilon^{-Y-3}\right)+O\bigg(e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\bigg).

(144)

Proof.

Let
p_{J,h}^{\pm}
be the density of
\pm J^{\prime}_{h}
under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
, and observe

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{F}p_{J,h}^{\pm}\big)(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\Big(c_{1}|u|^{Y}h\,{\pm}\,ic_{2}|u|^{Y}h\,\text{sgn}(u)\mp iu\gamma_{0}h\Big),

where

\displaystyle c_{1}:=(C_{+}\!+C_{-})\,\cos\bigg(\frac{\pi Y}{2}\bigg)\Gamma(-Y)<0,

\displaystyle c_{2}:=(C_{-}-C_{+})\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi Y}{2}\bigg)\Gamma(-Y).

(145)

Also let

\displaystyle\psi(x)

\displaystyle:=\bigg(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\phi\bigg(\frac{\cdot}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}-\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\bigg)(x)=\frac{\sigma\sqrt{h}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\bigg(i\varepsilon x-\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}x^{2}h\bigg).

Then, using Plancherel,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}\psi)(z)p_{J,h}^{\mp}(z)\,dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(u)\mathcal{F}\big(p_{J,h}^{\mp}(z)\big)(u)\,du

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma\sqrt{h}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{c_{1}u^{Y}h-\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}u^{2}h}\,\cos\left(\mp c_{2}u^{Y}h+u(\varepsilon\pm{\gamma}_{0}h)\right)du

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}g_{1}(\omega^{Y}h^{1-\frac{Y}{2}})e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}\cos\bigg(\frac{\omega\varepsilon^{\pm}_{0}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)d\omega

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\int_{0}^{\infty}g^{\pm}_{2}(\omega^{Y}h^{1-\frac{Y}{2}})e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}\sin\bigg(\frac{\omega\varepsilon^{\pm}_{0}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)d\omega

\displaystyle=:I_{1}-I_{2},

(146)

where
\varepsilon^{\pm}_{0}=\varepsilon\pm{\gamma_{0}}h,
and

g_{1}(u)=\frac{1}{\pi}e^{c_{1}u/\sigma^{Y}}\cos(c_{2}u/\sigma^{Y}),\quad g^{\pm}_{2}(u)=\mp\frac{1}{\pi}e^{c_{1}u/\sigma^{Y}}\sin(c_{2}u/\sigma^{Y}).

Fix
\delta>0
and set
D_{\delta,h}:=\{w\in(0,\infty):w^{Y}h^{1-Y/2}<(2\delta)^{Y/2}\}
.

I_{1}=\left(\int_{D_{\delta,h}}+\int_{D_{\delta,h}^{c}}\right)g_{1}(\omega^{Y}h^{1-\frac{Y}{2}})e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}\cos\bigg(\omega\frac{\varepsilon^{\pm}_{0}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)d\omega=:I_{11}+I_{12}.

Clearly, since
c_{1}<0
,

|I_{12}|\leq{}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{(2\delta)^{1/2}/h^{1/Y-1/2}}^{\infty}e^{-w^{2}/2}dw=O\left(h^{\frac{2-Y}{2Y}}e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\right).

We decompose the integral on
D_{\delta,h}
as follows:

\displaystyle\qquad I_{11}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{1}{j!}g_{1}^{(j)}(0)h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}\int_{0}^{\infty}w^{jY}e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}\cos\bigg(\omega\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)d\omega

(147)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{1}{j!}g_{1}^{(j)}(0)h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}\int_{(2\delta)^{1/2}/h^{1/Y-1/2}}^{\infty}w^{jY}e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}\cos\bigg(\omega\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)d\omega

(148)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{h^{m(1-\frac{Y}{2})}}{m!}\int_{0}^{(2\delta)^{1/2}/h^{1/Y-1/2}}g_{1}^{(m)}(\theta_{w})w^{mY}e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}\cos\bigg(\omega\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)d\omega,

(149)

where
\theta_{\omega}\in(0,(2\delta)^{Y/2})
. The
j^{th}
term in (
148
) is

O\left(h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}\int_{(2\delta)^{1/2}/h^{1/Y-1/2}}^{\infty}w^{jY}e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}d\omega\right)=O\left(h^{\frac{2-Y}{2Y}}e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\right),

where above we expressed the integral in terms of the incomplete gamma function and then apply the standard asymptotics behavior for such a function (e.g.,
(
Gradshteyn and Ryzhik 2007
, Section 8.35)
). For (
147
), the term corresponding to
j=0
is clearly
O(e^{-\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma^{2}h)})
. For
j\geq{}1
, we can apply the same argument as in (A.11) of
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
(see also
(
Frank W. J. Olver and Clark 2010
, expression 13.7.2)
) to show that

\displaystyle h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}\int_{0}^{\infty}\omega^{jY}\cos\bigg(\omega\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)e^{-\omega^{2}/2}\,d\omega

(150)

\displaystyle=h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}2^{(jY-1)/2}\,\Gamma\bigg(\frac{jY+1}{2}\bigg)M\bigg(\frac{jY+1}{2},\frac{1}{2},-\frac{(\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}\bigg)

\displaystyle=h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}2^{\frac{jY-1}{2}}\,\Gamma\bigg(\frac{jY+1}{2}\bigg)\left(\frac{\Gamma(1/2)}{\Gamma\big(-jY/2\big)}\bigg(\frac{(\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}\bigg)^{-\frac{jY+1}{2}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{h}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\left.+\frac{\Gamma(1/2)\,e^{-\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma^{2}h)}}{\Gamma\big((jY+1)/2\big)}\bigg(\frac{(\varepsilon^{\pm}_{0})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}\bigg)^{jY/2}\right)=O\bigg(\frac{h^{j+1/2}}{\varepsilon^{jY+1}}\bigg).

Therefore, the
j=1
term in (
147
) is the leading order term, and the second-order term therein is
O(h^{5/2}/\varepsilon^{2Y+1})
. Furthermore, by picking
m>5/(2-Y)
, we see the term in (
149
) – which is
O(h^{m(1-Y/2)})
– decays faster than the second-order term in (
147
). Putting together the above estimates and noting that
g_{1}^{\prime}(0)=c_{1}/(\pi\sigma^{Y})
, we conclude that

I_{1}=\frac{c_{1}}{\pi}\sigma 2^{Y}\frac{\Gamma(Y/2+1/2)\Gamma(1/2)}{\Gamma(-Y/2)}\frac{h^{3/2}}{(\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm})^{Y+1}}+O\left(e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\bigg(\frac{h^{5/2}}{\varepsilon^{Y+3}}\bigg),

where above we used that
h^{\frac{2-Y}{2Y}}e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\ll h^{5/2}\varepsilon^{-(Y+3)}
. Applying the same analysis to the term
I_{2}
in (
146
)

I_{2}=\pm\frac{c_{2}}{\pi}\sigma 2^{Y+1}\frac{\Gamma(Y/2+1)\Gamma(3/2)}{\Gamma((1-Y)/2)}\frac{h^{3/2}}{(\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm})^{Y+1}}+O\left(e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+{O\bigg(\frac{h^{5/2}}{\varepsilon^{Y+3}}\bigg)}.

We then get that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma 2^{Y}}{\pi}\left(c_{1}\frac{\Gamma(Y/2+1/2)\Gamma(1/2)}{\Gamma(-Y/2)}\,\mp\,c_{2}\frac{2\Gamma(Y/2+1)\Gamma(3/2)}{\Gamma((1-Y)/2)}\right)\frac{h^{3/2}}{(\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm})^{Y+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\left(e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+{O\bigg(\frac{h^{5/2}}{\varepsilon^{Y+3}}\bigg)}.

By applying the properties

\Gamma(x)\,\Gamma\left(x+1/2\right)=\sqrt{\pi}\,2^{1-2x}\,\Gamma\left(2x\right)

and
\Gamma(1-x)\,\Gamma(x)=\frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi x)}
, we can simplify the coefficients as

\displaystyle\frac{2^{Y}}{\pi}c_{1}\frac{\Gamma(Y/2+1/2)\Gamma(1/2)}{\Gamma(-Y/2)}=\frac{C_{+}+C_{-}}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{2^{Y+1}}{\pi}c_{2}\frac{\Gamma(Y/2+1)\Gamma(3/2)}{\Gamma((1-Y)/2)}=\frac{C_{-}-C_{+}}{2}.

We finally obtain (
144
).
∎

The following lemma is used in the proofs of Lemmas
15
and
19
.

Lemma 14
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
, fix
\gamma_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, any integer
k\geq 0
, and let
J^{\prime}_{h}=S_{h}+\gamma_{0}h
. Then, with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon\ll 1
,as
h\to 0
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left(\mp J^{\prime}_{h}\right)^{k}\,\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{k-Y-1}\right)+{O\left(\varepsilon^{k}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)},

(151)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)^{k}\,\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{\frac{3-k}{2}}\varepsilon^{k-Y-1}\right)+O\left(\varepsilon^{k}h^{\frac{-k}{2}}\,e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

Proof.

We use the same notation as in the proof of Lemma
13
.
We first establish the asymptotic bound for any fixed
\ell=0,1,\dots
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\ell)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}u^{\ell}\exp{\left(iu(\varepsilon\pm\gamma_{0}h)-\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}u^{2}h+|u|^{Y}h\left(c_{1}\,\mp\,ic_{2}{\rm sgn}(u)\right)\right)}du

\displaystyle=O\left(h\varepsilon^{-Y-\ell-1}\right)+O\left(h^{-\ell-\frac{1}{2}}\,\varepsilon^{\ell}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

(152)

We suppose
\ell\geq 0
is even (the case for
\ell
odd can be established similarly). As in (
146
), we can write

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\ell)

\displaystyle=2\,(\sigma\sqrt{h})^{-\ell-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}w^{\ell}g_{1}(h^{1-Y/2}\omega^{Y})e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}\cos\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\omega\right)d\omega

\displaystyle\quad-2\,(\sigma\sqrt{h})^{-\ell-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}w^{\ell}g_{2}^{\pm}(h^{1-Y/2}\omega^{Y})e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}\sin\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\omega\right)d\omega.

(153)

Applying the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
13
, we can show that, for
\delta>0
and positive integer
m
,

\displaystyle\qquad\;\mathcal{I}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}O\left(h^{-\frac{\ell+1}{2}}h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}\int_{(2\delta)^{1/2}/h^{1/Y-1/2}}^{\infty}w^{jY+\ell}e^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}d\omega\right)

(154)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}O\left(h^{-\frac{\ell+1}{2}}h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}\int_{0}^{\infty}\omega^{jY+\ell}\cos\bigg(\omega\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)e^{-\omega^{2}/2}\,d\omega\right)

(155)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}O\left(h^{-\frac{\ell+1}{2}}h^{j(1-\frac{Y}{2})}\int_{0}^{\infty}\omega^{jY+\ell}\sin\bigg(\omega\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{\pm}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)e^{-\omega^{2}/2}\,d\omega\right)

(156)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{-\frac{\ell+1}{2}}h^{m(1-Y/2)}\right)+O\left(h^{-\ell-\frac{1}{2}}\,\varepsilon^{\ell}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

(157)

Each of the
m
terms in (
154
) is
O(h^{(1-Y-\ell)/Y}e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}})
. For the terms in (
155
)-(
156
), we recall the estimates (cf (43) and (44) in
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
):

\displaystyle h^{j(1-Y/2)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\omega^{jY+r}\cos\bigg(\omega\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)e^{-\omega^{2}/2}\,d\omega=O\bigg(\frac{h^{j+(r+1)/2}}{\varepsilon^{jY+r+1}}\bigg),

\displaystyle h^{j(1-Y/2)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\omega^{jY+r}\sin\bigg(\omega\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)e^{-\omega^{2}/2}\,d\omega=O\bigg(\frac{h^{j+(r+1)/2}}{\varepsilon^{jY+r+1}}\bigg),

valid when
h\to{}0
and
\varepsilon/\sqrt{h}\to{}\infty
. Then, we can conclude that the leading term in (
155
) and (
156
) corresponds to
j=1
and is of order
O\left(h\varepsilon^{-Y-\ell-1}\right)
, which is asymptotically larger than every term in (
154
). The first term in (
157
) can be made of higher order by choosing
m
large enough. We then conclude (
152
).

Turning to (
151
),
we apply Plancherel to get:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left(\mp J^{\prime}_{h}\right)^{k}\,\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}\psi)(z)z^{k}p_{J,h}^{\mp}(z)\,dz=(-i)^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi^{(k)}(u)\mathcal{F}\big(p_{J,h}^{\mp}(z)\big)(u)\,du

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon^{k}\,\frac{\sigma\sqrt{h}}{2\pi}\,\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{m=0}^{\left\lfloor l/2\right\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{l+m}\,2^{l-2m}\,k!}{(k-l)!\,m!\,(l-2m)!}\left(i\varepsilon\right)^{-l}\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}h}{2}\right)^{l-m}\mathcal{I}({l-2m})

\displaystyle\quad=O\left(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{k-Y-1}\right)+O\left(\varepsilon^{k}\,e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(\varepsilon^{k}h^{\frac{2-Y}{2Y}}\,e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\right),

(158)

where on the last line we made use of the asymptotic expression (
152
) for
\mathcal{I}(\ell)
. This establishes (
151
).
For the second asymptotic bound, based on (
158
), we can compute

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)^{k}\,\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=(\sigma\sqrt{h})^{-k}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{k}\binom{k}{m}\varepsilon^{k-m}(\pm J^{\prime}_{h})^{m}\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J^{\prime}_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=(\sigma\sqrt{h})^{-k}\sum_{m=0}^{k}\binom{k}{m}\varepsilon^{k-m}\bigg[O\left(h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{m-Y-1}\right)+O\left(\varepsilon^{m}\,e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+O\left(\varepsilon^{m}h^{\frac{2-Y}{2Y}}\,e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\right)\bigg].

This clearly implies the result.
∎

The following Lemma is used in the proof of Proposition
5
.

Lemma 15
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
. Fix a positive integer
k
. Then, as
h\to 0
,
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon\ll 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(W_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle~={(2k-1)!!\,h^{k}+O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}\right)+o\left(h^{k+1/Y}\right)+O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{2k-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)}.

Proof.

The case of
k=1
has been proved in the Step 1 of Lemma 3.1 of
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
. Following a similar procedure, we generalize to the case of
k\geq 1
. Note

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(W_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=(2k-1)!!\,h^{k}-\mathbb{E}\Big(W_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=(2k-1)!!\,h^{k}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}-\eta h}\,W_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=(2k-1)!!\,h^{k}-h^{k}e^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{1}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-h^{k}e^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\Big)W_{1}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|>\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=:(2k-1)!!\,h^{k}-h^{k}e^{-\eta h}I_{1}(h)-h^{k}e^{-\eta h}I_{2}(h).

(159)

We first analyze the asymptotic behavior of
I_{1}(h)
. Write

\displaystyle I_{1}(h)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{1}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}>\varepsilon\}}\Big)+\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{1}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}-J_{h}>\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=:I_{1}^{+}(h)+I_{1}^{-}(h).

(160)

Recall the notation
\phi(x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-x^{2}/2}
, and
\overline{\Phi}(x):=\int_{x}^{\infty}\phi(x)\,dx
.
By repeating integration by parts, we have for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(W_{1}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{W_{1}>x\}}\right)=(2k-1)!!\,\overline{\Phi}(x)+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{(2k-1)!!}{(2j+1)!!}\,x^{2j+1}\phi(x).

Also, note that expression (A.21) of
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
shows

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\pm\frac{J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)=O\Big(e^{-\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma^{2}h)}\Big)+O\big(h\varepsilon^{-Y}\big),\quad\text{as }\,h\rightarrow 0.

(161)

Thus, by conditioning on
J_{h}
and then applying Lemma
14
,

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\pm}(h)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg((2k-1)!!\,\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\mp\frac{J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{(2k-1)!!}{(2j+1)!!}\,\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\mp\frac{J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)^{2j+1}\phi\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\mp\frac{J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=O\Big(e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\Big)+O\big(h\varepsilon^{-Y}\big)+O\left(h^{2-k}\varepsilon^{2k-2-Y}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}-k}\varepsilon^{2k-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h^{\frac{1}{Y}-k}\varepsilon^{2k-1}e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\right)

=O\left(h^{2-k}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}\right)+O\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}-k}\varepsilon^{2k-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h^{\frac{1}{Y}-k}\varepsilon^{2k-1}e^{-\delta h^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\right).

(162)

Using the same procedure as shown in the Step 1.2 of Theorem 3.1 of
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
, we have that
I_{2}(h)=o(h^{1/Y})
. Note that the last term (
162
) is of smaller order than the first term in (
162
). The result then follows.
∎

The lemma below gives an expansion for a type of truncated higher-order moment of a pure-jump stable Lévy process.
This is used in the proofs of Propositions
3
and
4
.

Lemma 16
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
. Let
\gamma_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\sigma\in[0,\infty)
, and consider
S_{h}
as in (
64
). Then, for any fixed
k\geq 1
, as
h\to 0
with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon\ll 1
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(S_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+h\gamma_{0}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{+}\!+\!C_{-}}{2k-Y}h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}+\frac{\sigma^{2}(C_{+}+C_{-})(2k-1-Y)}{2}h^{2}\,\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{2k-4-Y}\right)+O\big(h^{2}\,\varepsilon^{2k-2Y}\big)+O\left(\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y}h^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

(163)

Proof.

It will be evident from the proof below that the statement remains valid for the case
\sigma=0
. However, for brevity, we only give the details for the case
\sigma>0
, which is more involved. First suppose
\gamma_{0}=0
. For simplicity, let
I(x)=({\frac{-\varepsilon-x\sigma\sqrt{h}}{h^{1/Y}}},{\frac{\varepsilon-x\sigma\sqrt{h}}{h^{1/Y}}})
and also let
r:=r(h):=\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\to\infty
, as
h\to 0
. Then, in terms of the density
p_{S}
of
S_{1}
under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|{\sigma W_{h}}+{S_{h}}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle={h^{2k/Y}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{1}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+h^{1/Y}S_{1}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)}

\displaystyle=h^{2k/Y}\Big\{\int_{\{|x|<r\}}+\int_{\{|x|>r\}}\Big\}\int_{I(x)}u^{2k}p_{S}(u)du\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle=:h^{2k/Y}(T_{1}+T_{2}).

(164)

We first focus on
T_{1}
. Write
\mathcal{D}(u):=p_{S}(u)-(C_{-}\mathbf{1}_{\{u<0\}}+C_{+}\mathbf{1}_{\{u>0\}})|u|^{-1-Y}
, recall
\bar{C}=C_{+}+C_{-}
, and observe

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{C}}{2k-Y}(\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2k-Y}\int_{-r}^{r}\Big(1-\frac{x}{r}\Big)^{2k-Y}\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{I(x)}u^{2k}\mathcal{D}(u)du\phi(x)dx.

(165)

Letting
f(u)=(1-u)^{2k-Y}
,
q_{m}(u)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{m}\frac{f^{(\ell)}(0)}{\ell!}u^{\ell}
, and
R_{m}(u)=f(u)-q_{m}(u)
,
the integral in first term of (
165
) may then be written as

\displaystyle\int_{-r}^{r}\Big(1-\frac{x}{r}\Big)^{2k-Y}\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle~=\Big\{\int_{-r/2}^{r/2}+\int_{(-r,r)\cap{(-r/2,r/2)^{c}}}\Big\}f(x/r)\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle~=\int_{-r/2}^{r/2}q_{m}(x/r)\phi(x)dx+\int_{-r/2}^{r/2}R_{m}(x/r)\phi(x)dx+O(r^{-1}e^{-r^{2}/8}).

For
|u|<\frac{1}{2}
, there is a constant
K^{\prime}=K^{\prime}({m},Y)
such that
|R_{m}(u)|\leq K^{\prime}|u|^{{m}+1}
. Thus,

\int_{-r/2}^{r/2}|R_{m}(x/r)|\phi(x)dx\leq K^{\prime}r^{-({m}+1)}\int_{-r/2}^{r/2}x^{{m}+1}\phi(x)dx=O(r^{-({m}+1)}).

Moreover, using that
\int_{r/2}^{\infty}x^{m}\phi(x)dx=O(r^{({m}-1)}e^{-(r/2)^{2}/2})
,

\int_{-r/2}^{r/2}q_{m}(x/r)\phi(x)dx=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}q_{m}(x/r)\phi(x)dx+O(r^{({m}-1)}e^{-r^{2}/8}).

Hence, taking
{m}=3
, as
r\to\infty,

\displaystyle\qquad\int_{-r}^{r}\Big(1-\frac{x}{r}\Big)^{2k-Y}\phi(x)dx=1+\frac{(2k-Y)(2k-1-Y)}{2}\frac{1}{r^{2}}+O(r^{-4}).

(166)

To study the second term on the right-hand side of (
165
), we consider the cases
k=1
and
k>1
separately. First suppose
k=1
. We first show

\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{I(x)}u^{2}\mathcal{D}(u)du\phi(x)dx=O((\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2-2Y}).

(167)

When
Y<1
, (
167
) follows from a straightforward computation, so we provide details only for
Y>1
. In this case, by applying Lemma
20
and using the symmetry of
\phi
,

\displaystyle\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{I(x)}u^{2}\mathcal{D}(u)du\phi(x)dx=-\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-b(x),b(x))}u^{2}\mathcal{D}(u)du\,\phi(x)dx,

(168)

where
b(x)=\frac{\varepsilon+x\sigma\sqrt{h}}{h^{1/Y}}=\frac{\varepsilon(1+x/r)}{h^{1/Y}}\in(0,2\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})
.
Observe

0<b(x)<1
if and only if
x\in\big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}},\frac{h^{1/Y}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\big)=\big(-r,r(\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)\big)
. So, we consider

\displaystyle\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{b(x)}^{\infty}u^{2}|\mathcal{D}(u)|du\,\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{-r}^{r(\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)}+\int_{r(\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)}^{r}\right)\int_{b(x)}^{\infty}u^{2}|\mathcal{D}(u)|du\,\phi(x)dx.

Applying (
142
), the first integral above can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{-r}^{(\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)r}\int_{b(x)}^{\infty}u^{2}|\mathcal{D}(u)|du\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq K\int_{-r}^{(\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)r}\bigg(\int_{b(x)}^{1}u^{1-Y}du+\int_{1}^{\infty}u^{1-2Y}du\bigg)\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq K\int_{-r}^{(\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)r}\Big(\frac{1}{2-Y}+\frac{1}{|2-2Y|}\Big)\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq K\int_{-r}^{-r/2}\phi(x)dx\leq O(r^{-1}e^{-(r/2)^{2}/2}).

(169)

Now, for
x\in((\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)r,r)
,
b(x)>1
and, so, again applying (
142
),

\displaystyle\int_{(\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)r}^{r}\int_{b(x)}^{\infty}u^{2}|\mathcal{D}(u)|du\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{-r(1-\varepsilon^{-1}h^{1/Y})}^{\infty}\int_{b(x)}^{\infty}u^{2}|\mathcal{D}(u)|du\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq K(\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2-2Y}\int_{(\frac{h^{1/Y}}{\varepsilon}-1)r}^{\infty}\Big(1+\frac{x}{r}\Big)^{2-2Y}\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle=O((\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2-2Y})

(170)

Expressions (
169
)-(
170
) show that

\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{b(x)}^{\infty}u^{2}|\mathcal{D}(u)|du\,\phi(x)dx=O((\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2-2Y}).

Similarly,
\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{-\infty}^{-b(x)}u^{2}|\mathcal{D}(u)|du\,\phi(x)dx=O((\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2-2Y})
and thus (
167
) holds.
For the case
k>1
, using (
142
),

\displaystyle\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{I(x)}u^{2k}|\mathcal{D}(u)|du\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq K\int_{-r}^{r}\int_{I(x)}u^{2k-2Y-1}du\phi(x)dx=O((\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2k-2Y}).

(171)

Plugging (
166
), (
167
), and (
171
) into
(
165
), we conclude that for
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{C}}{2k-Y}\varepsilon^{2k-Y}h^{1-\frac{2k}{Y}}\Big(1+\frac{(2k-Y)(2k-1-Y)}{2}\frac{1}{r^{2}}+O(r^{-4})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+O((\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2k-2Y}).

(172)

Now we examine
T_{2}
in (
164
).
Again applying (
142
),

\displaystyle\int_{r}^{\infty}\int_{\frac{-\varepsilon-x\sigma\sqrt{h}}{h^{1/Y}}}^{\frac{\varepsilon-x\sigma\sqrt{h}}{h^{1/Y}}}

\displaystyle|{u^{2k}}p_{S}(u)\phi(x)|du\,dx

\displaystyle\leq K(\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2k-Y}\int_{r}^{\infty}(1+x/r)^{2k-Y}\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq K(\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2k-Y}\int_{r}^{\infty}(2x/r)^{2k-Y}\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle=O\left((\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2k-Y}r^{-1}e^{-r^{2}/2}\right).

(173)

Analogously,

\int_{-\infty}^{-r}\int_{\frac{-\varepsilon-x\sigma\sqrt{h}}{h^{1/Y}}}^{\frac{\varepsilon-x\sigma\sqrt{h}}{h^{1/Y}}}|{u^{2k}}p_{S}(u)\phi(x)|du\,dx=O\left((\varepsilon h^{-1/Y})^{2k-Y}r^{-1}e^{-r^{2}/2}\right).

Thus, based on (
164
), (
172
), and (
173
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(S_{h}^{2k}\,

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

(174)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{C}}{2k-Y}h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}+\frac{\bar{C}(2k-1-Y)}{2}\sigma^{2}{h^{2}\,\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}}+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{2k-4-Y}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O\big(h^{2}\,\varepsilon^{2k-2Y}\big)+O\left(\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y}h^{3/2}e^{-\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma^{2}h)}\right).

This completes the case
\gamma_{0}=0
. For
\gamma_{0}\neq 0
, take
h>0
small enough so that
|\gamma_{0}|h<\varepsilon
, and observe

\displaystyle\left|{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+h\gamma_{0}|\leq\varepsilon\}}-{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon-h|\gamma_{0}|<|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon<|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon+h|\gamma_{0}|\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon-h|\gamma_{0}|<|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon+h|\gamma_{0}|\}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\left(S_{h}^{2k}\,\left|{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+h\gamma_{0}|\leq\varepsilon\}}-{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2h^{2k/Y}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(S_{1}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon-h|\gamma_{0}|<|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon+h|\gamma_{0}|\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2k/Y}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg\{\int_{b(x)-\gamma_{0}h^{1-1/Y}}^{b(x)+\gamma_{0}h^{1-1/Y}}+\int_{-b(x)-\gamma_{0}h^{1-1/Y}}^{-b(x)+\gamma_{0}h^{1-1/Y}}\bigg\}u^{2k}p_{S}(u)du\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2k/Y}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{b(x)-\gamma_{0}h^{1-1/Y}}^{b(x)+\gamma_{0}h^{1-1/Y}}|u|^{2k-1-Y}du\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(1+\frac{x}{r}+o(1)\Big)^{2k-Y-1}\phi(x)dx

\displaystyle=O(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-1})=o(h^{2}\,\varepsilon^{2k-2Y}).

Hence,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(

\displaystyle S_{h}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+h\gamma_{0}|\leq\varepsilon\}})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(S_{h}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}})+\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(S_{h}^{2k}\,\left({\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+h\gamma_{0}|\leq\varepsilon\}}-{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(S_{h}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}})+o(h^{2}\,\varepsilon^{2k-2Y}).

In view of (
174
), this implies the result for arbitrary
\gamma_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
∎

The lemma below gives an expansion for the truncated higher-order moments of the pure-jump tempered stable Lévy process
J
.
This lemma is similar to a result used in Step 2 of the proof of Theorem 3.1 of
Figueroa-López et al. 2022
; here we provide a sharper result and generalize it to an arbitrary order
k\geq 1
.

Lemma 17
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
. Let
k\geq 1
be an integer. Then, for any
\sigma\in[0,\infty)
, as
h\to 0
with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon\ll 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(J_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{+}\!+\!C_{-}}{2k-Y}h\varepsilon^{2k-Y}+O\big(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y-2}\big)+O\big(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y/2}\big),

(175)

and when
\sigma=0
, for every
p\geq 1
(not necessarily integer),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(|J_{h}|^{p}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)\leq Kh\varepsilon^{p-Y}.

(176)

Proof.

We begin with (
175
). It will be evident from the proof below that the statement remains valid for the case
\sigma=0
. However, for brevity, we only give the details for the case
\sigma>0
, which is more involved. Recall
J_{h}=S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h
, where
S_{h}
is
Y
-stable under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(J_{h}^{2k}\,

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}-\eta h}\,J_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=e^{-\eta h}\,\sum_{j=0}^{{2k}}\frac{(2k)!}{j!\,(2k-j)!}(\widetilde{\gamma}h)^{2k-j}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}S_{h}^{j}{\bf 1}_{\{|W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big).

Now, denoting
\widetilde{C}_{\ell}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}}\big(e^{-\ell\varphi(x)}-1+\ell\varphi(x)\big)\tilde{\nu}(dx)
,
\ell=1,2
, by Assumption
3
-(v) we have

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\big)^{2}\right)=e^{\widetilde{C}_{2}h}-2e^{\widetilde{C}_{1}h}+1\sim\big(\widetilde{C}_{2}-2\widetilde{C}_{1}\big)h,\quad\text{as }\,h\rightarrow 0.

(177)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}

\displaystyle S_{h}^{j}{\bf 1}_{\{|W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{j}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)+\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\big)S_{h}^{j}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2j}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\bigg)^{1/2}\left(1+\Big(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\big)^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2j}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\bigg)^{1/2}\left(1+O\left(h^{1/2}\right)\right),\quad h\to 0.

(178)

In particular, applying Lemma
16
with
\gamma_{0}=\widetilde{\gamma}
, based on expression (
178
), for any
0\leq j\leq 2k-1
, we see that

\displaystyle h^{2k-j}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}S_{h}^{j}{\bf 1}_{\{|W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=h^{2k-j}O\left(\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}S_{h}^{2j}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)^{1/2}\right)=O(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y/2}).

Thus, by Lemma
16
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(J_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=e^{-\eta h}\,\sum_{j=0}^{{2k}}\frac{(2k)!}{j!\,(2k-j)!}(\widetilde{\gamma}h)^{2k-j}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}S_{h}^{j}{\bf 1}_{\{|W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=e^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}S_{h}^{2k}{\bf 1}_{\{|W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)+O(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y/2})

\displaystyle=e^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+e^{-\eta h}\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\Big)S_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)+O\left(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y/2}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\eta h}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y/2}\right)\cdot O\left(h^{1/2}\right)+O\left(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\big(1+O(h)\big)\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)+O\left(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y/2}\right),

where in the last line we used that
h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{2k-1-Y/2}\ll h\varepsilon^{2k-Y/2}
since
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon
, and on the second-to-last line we applied Lemma
16
. The statement (
175
) follows, since
h\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)=O(h^{2}\varepsilon^{2k-Y})=o\left(h\varepsilon^{2k-Y/2}\right)
by Lemma
16
.

The statement (
176
) for
p\geq 2
follows from the first statement; indeed, when
p=2k+s
for
0<s<2
, (
176
) follows from the bound
|J_{h}|^{p}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\leq\varepsilon^{s}|J_{h}|^{2k}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}
. The general case
p\geq 1
can be established in a manner similar to the proof of Lemma
58
and is omitted.
∎

The next lemma gives a higher-order expansion for the right-tail of a Lévy process with a stable Lévy component of unbounded variation. This result is used in the proof of Proposition
3
.

Lemma 18
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
, and let

\displaystyle P_{n}:=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}\geq{}\varepsilon\right)=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\pm\frac{S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right).

Then, as
h\to 0
with
\sqrt{h}\ll\varepsilon\ll 1
,

\displaystyle P_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\mp}}{Y}h\varepsilon^{-Y}+C_{\mp}(1+Y)\sigma^{2}h^{2}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}+

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C_{\mp}(1+Y)(2+Y)(3+Y)}{8}h^{3}\varepsilon^{-4-Y}+O\left(h^{4}\varepsilon^{-Y-6}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{-1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)\cdot\left(1+h\varepsilon^{-2}\right)+O(h\varepsilon^{2-2Y})\cdot(1+h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-1}+h\varepsilon^{-2})

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h\varepsilon^{3-2Y-Y/2}\right)+O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

Proof.

Fix
0<\bar{\delta}<1/32
. Let
S^{\bar{\delta}}
be a Lévy process with no diffusion part, Lévy measure
\widetilde{\nu}(dz){\bf 1}_{|z|\leq\bar{\delta}\varepsilon}
, and third component of the characteristic triplet
\gamma_{\bar{\delta}}
(
\gamma_{\bar{\delta}}
to be specified momentarily). Let
N^{\bar{\delta}}
a standard Poisson process with intensity
\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}:=\widetilde{\nu}(\{z:|z|>{\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon\})
,
(\xi_{i}^{\bar{\delta}})_{i\geq 1}
