be a sequence of i.i.d. random variables with probability distribution
\widetilde{\nu}(dz){\bf 1}_{|z|>\bar{\delta}\varepsilon}/\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}
(again under both
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
and
\mathbb{P}
).
We also assume that
S^{\bar{\delta}}
,
N^{\bar{\delta}}
, and
(\xi_{i}^{\bar{\delta}})_{i\geq 1}
are independent. Then, by construction, under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
, we may take
\gamma_{\bar{\delta}}
so that
S_{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{=}}S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}+\sum_{i=1}^{N_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}}\xi_{i}^{\bar{\delta}}
.
Therefore, we can express
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(\overline{\Phi}((\varepsilon\pm S_{h})/{\sigma\sqrt{h}}))=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}\geq\varepsilon\right)=\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\mp\sum_{i=1}^{N_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}}\xi_{i}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon)
and conditioning on
N_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\overline{\Phi}\bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\pm\frac{S_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h}\,\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h\,e^{-\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h}\,\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\mp\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right)

(179)

\displaystyle\quad+e^{-\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h}\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{(\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h\,)^{k}}{k!}\,\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\mp\sum_{i=1}^{k}\xi_{i}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right).

To estimate the first term in (
179
), by a Taylor approximation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\overline{\Phi}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\pm\frac{S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{\Phi}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\pm\overline{\Phi}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{(S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}h}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\overline{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)d\theta\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\geq\varepsilon\right)\,\mp\,\phi\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\frac{\gamma_{\bar{\delta}}h}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}

(180)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{h^{-3/2}}{\sigma^{3}(2\pi)^{1/2}}\,\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\,e^{-\frac{(\varepsilon\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}(\varepsilon\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})d\theta\right).

To estimate the last term in (
180
), first note that for any integer
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{k}\right)\sim h\int_{|z|\leq{\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon}z^{k}\widetilde{\nu}(dz)=O(h\varepsilon^{k-Y}).

Since
\bar{\delta}<1/32
, by similar arguments to those shown in expression (46) of
Mijatović and Tankov 2016
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}\left(|S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)\leq C_{0}(h\varepsilon^{-Y})^{4}
,
for some constant
C_{0}
.
Thus, we can compute

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}\int_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle(1-\theta)\,e^{-\frac{(\varepsilon\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}d\theta\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\,e^{-\frac{(\varepsilon\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}d\theta\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\,e^{-\frac{(\varepsilon\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}d\theta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}\right)\,e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}+\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(h\varepsilon^{2-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{4}\right)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}\left(|S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=O\left(h\varepsilon^{2-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h^{5/2}\varepsilon^{2-2Y-Y/2}\right).

(181)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{3}\int_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle(1-\theta)\theta e^{-\frac{(\varepsilon\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}d\theta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{3}\right)\,e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}+\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{3}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(h\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h^{5/2}\varepsilon^{3-2Y-Y/2}\right).

(182)

Then, based on (
180
), using
\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\geq\varepsilon\right)\pm\phi\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\frac{\gamma_{\bar{\delta}}h}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}=O(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}})
together with (
181
) and (
182
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\big)

(183)

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}h}}\right)+O\left(h\varepsilon^{3-2Y-Y/2}\right).

This establishes the asymptotic behavior of the first term in (
179
). For the second term in (
179
), again by a Taylor approximation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\big(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\mp\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\big)

(184)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right)\mp\mathbb{E}\left(\phi\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\right)\frac{\gamma_{\bar{\delta}}h}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma^{-3}h^{-3/2}(2\pi)^{-1/2}\,\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\,e^{-\frac{(\varepsilon\pm\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}(\varepsilon\pm\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\pm\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})d\theta\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right)+O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{1-Y}\right)+O\left(\varepsilon^{2-Y}\right),

(185)

where the last equality is because

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\,e^{-\frac{(\varepsilon\mp\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\mp\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}{|\varepsilon\mp\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\mp\theta S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}|}d\theta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\sqrt{2}\sup_{x}|x|e^{-x^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{h}\mathbb{E}\left((S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}})^{2}\right)=O(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{2-Y}).

For the first term in (
185
), note

\displaystyle\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}\mathbb{P}\big(

\displaystyle\sigma W_{h}+\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{-{\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\geq\varepsilon-z)\widetilde{\nu}(dz)+\int_{{\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\geq\varepsilon-z)\widetilde{\nu}(dz)

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}{\bf 1}_{\{|z|>{\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon\}}\widetilde{\nu}(dz)+\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}\mathbb{P}\left(\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right).

(186)

Now, for the first term in (
186
), we employ the tail bound
\mathbb{P}(|W_{1}|>x)\leq\frac{1}{x\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\{-x^{2}/2\}
:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{-{\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\geq\varepsilon-z)\widetilde{\nu}(dz)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma\sqrt{h}}{\varepsilon\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\cdot\frac{C_{-}}{Y}({\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon)^{-Y}

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

(187)

Then we estimate the second and the third term in (
186
),

\displaystyle\int_{{\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\geq\varepsilon-z)\widetilde{\nu}(dz)-\int_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}\widetilde{\nu}(dz)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bar{\delta}}\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\geq\varepsilon-z)\widetilde{\nu}(dz)-\int_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\leq\varepsilon-z)\widetilde{\nu}(dz)

\displaystyle=C_{+}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon(1-{\bar{\delta}})}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\geq u)\,(\varepsilon-u)^{-1-Y}du

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-C_{+}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\leq-u)\,(\varepsilon+u)^{-1-Y}du

\displaystyle=C_{+}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon(1-{\bar{\delta}})}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\geq u)\,\big((\varepsilon-u)^{-1-Y}-(\varepsilon+u)^{-1-Y}\big)du

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-C_{+}\int_{\varepsilon(1-{\bar{\delta}})}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\leq-u)\,(\varepsilon+u)^{-1-Y}du

\displaystyle=:T_{1}-T_{2}.

(188)

For the first term
T_{1}
in (
188
), for simplicity let
r=r(h)=\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\to\infty
as
h\to 0
, and also let
f(u)=(1-u)^{-1-Y}-(1+u)^{-1-Y}
. Also, recall
\mathbb{E}\left(|W_{1}|^{k+1}\right)=2k\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{k}\mathbb{P}\left(W_{1}>x\right)\,dx
. By a 4th order Taylor approximation of
f(u)
at
u=0
,

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=C_{+}\sigma h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1-Y}\int_{0}^{r(1-{\bar{\delta}})}\mathbb{P}(W_{1}\geq x)f(x/r)dx

\displaystyle=C_{+}\sigma h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1-Y}\int_{0}^{r(1-{\bar{\delta}})}\mathbb{P}(W_{1}\geq x)\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{4}\frac{f^{(k)}(0)x^{k}}{r^{k}\cdot k!}+\frac{f^{(5)}(\vartheta(x))x^{5}}{r^{5}\cdot 5!}\Big)dx

\displaystyle=C_{+}\sigma h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1-Y}\Bigg(\frac{f^{\prime}(0)\mathbb{E}W_{1}^{2}}{r}+\frac{f^{(3)}(0)\mathbb{E}W_{1}^{4}}{r^{3}\cdot 3!}+O(r^{-5})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-O\Big(\frac{1}{r}\int_{r(1-{\bar{\delta}})}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(W_{1}\geq x)dx\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=C_{+}(1+Y)\sigma^{2}h\varepsilon^{-2-Y}+\frac{C_{+}(1+Y)(2+Y)(3+Y)}{8}\sigma^{4}h^{2}\varepsilon^{-4-Y}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-6}\right)-O\left(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{-3-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}(1-\delta)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

(189)

The second term in (
188
) can be estimated as

\displaystyle|T_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{+}\,\mathbb{P}(\sigma W_{h}\leq\varepsilon({\bar{\delta}}-1))\int_{\varepsilon(1-{\bar{\delta}})}^{\infty}\,(\varepsilon+u)^{-1-Y}du

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}(1-\delta)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right).

(190)

Finally, for the fourth term in (
186
),

\displaystyle\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}\mathbb{P}\left(\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right)=\int_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}\widetilde{\nu}(dz)=\frac{C_{+}}{Y}\varepsilon^{-Y}

(191)

Then, combining (
187
), (
189
), (
190
), and (
191
), and by repeating analogous arguments for
\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}-\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right)
, based on (
186
) we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma W_{h}\mp\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\mp}}{Y}\varepsilon^{-Y}+C_{\mp}(1+Y)\sigma^{2}h\varepsilon^{-2-Y}+\frac{C_{\mp}(1+Y)(2+Y)(3+Y)}{8}\sigma^{4}h^{2}\varepsilon^{-4-Y}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+O\left(h^{3}\varepsilon^{-Y-6}\right)+O\left(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)\cdot\left(1+h\varepsilon^{-2}\right).

Therefore, by (
185
), and using that
\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}=O(\varepsilon^{-Y})
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}

\displaystyle h\,e^{-\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h}\,\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\mp\xi_{1}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right)

(192)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\mp}}{Y}h\varepsilon^{-Y}+C_{\mp}(1+Y)\sigma^{2}h^{2}\varepsilon^{-2-Y}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C_{\mp}(1+Y)(2+Y)(3+Y)}{8}\sigma^{4}h^{3}\varepsilon^{-4-Y}+O\left(h^{4}\varepsilon^{-Y-6}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{-1-Y}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\right)\cdot\left(1+h\varepsilon^{-2}\right)+{O(h\varepsilon^{2-2Y})\cdot(1+h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1})}.

This establishes the asymptotic behavior of the second term in (
179
). For the third term in (
179
), simply note

e^{-\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h}\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{(\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h\,)^{k}}{k!}\,\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma W_{h}\mp S_{h}^{\bar{\delta}}\mp\sum_{i=1}^{k}\xi_{i}^{\bar{\delta}}\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{(\lambda_{\bar{\delta}}h\,)^{k}}{k!}=O(h^{2}\varepsilon^{-2Y}).

(193)

Finally, combining (
183
), (
192
) and (
193
), based on (
179
) we obtain the result.
∎

The following lemma is used in the proof of Proposition
5
.

Lemma 19
.

Suppose
Y\in(0,1)\cup(1,2)
.
Assume as
h\to 0
,
\varepsilon\to 0
with
\varepsilon\gg h^{1/2-s}
for some
s>0
.
Let
a,b\geq 1
be integers. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(W_{h}^{a}\,J_{h}^{b}

\displaystyle\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{cases}O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{a+b-Y-2}\right)+O\big(h^{1+a/2}\varepsilon^{b-Y/2}\big),&a\text{ odd},\\
O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{a+b-Y-2}\right)+O(h^{1+a/2}\varepsilon^{b-Y}),&a,b\text{ even},\\
O\left(h^{2}\varepsilon^{a+b-Y-2}\right)+O(h^{\frac{1+a}{2}}\varepsilon^{b-Y/2}),&a\text{ even},b\text{ odd}.\end{cases}

Proof.

First, we decompose
\mathbb{E}\left(W_{h}^{a}\,J_{h}^{b}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(

\displaystyle W_{h}^{a}\,J_{h}^{b}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\big)

\displaystyle=h^{a/2}e^{-\eta h}\Big[\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(W_{1}^{a}\,J_{h}^{b}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}+\big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\big)\,W_{1}^{a}\,J_{h}^{b}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=:h^{a/2}e^{-\eta h}\big(T_{1}(h)+T_{2}(h)\big).

(194)

We first analyze the asymptotic behavior of
T_{1}(h)
.
Note that by repeating integration by parts, we have for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
x_{1}<x_{2}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\big(W_{1}^{a}

\displaystyle\,{\bf 1}_{\{x_{1}<W_{1}<x_{2}\}}\big)

(195)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sum_{j=0}^{d}\frac{(a-1)!!}{(2j)!!}\left(x_{1}^{2j}\phi(x_{1})-x_{2}^{2j}\phi(x_{2})\right),&a=2d+1,\\
\begin{array}[]{l}\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\frac{(a-1)!!}{(2j+1)!!}\left(x_{1}^{2j+1}\phi(x_{1})-x_{2}^{2j+1}\phi(x_{2})\right)\\
\quad+(a-1)!!\,\left(\overline{\Phi}(x_{1})-\overline{\Phi}(x_{2})\right),\end{array}&a=2d,\end{cases}

where
d\geq 0
is an integer.
However, by applying Lemma
14
and noting that under the assumption
\varepsilon\gg h^{1/2-s}
,
\sqrt{h}\varepsilon^{j}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}h}}\ll h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{j-Y-1}
and
\sqrt{h}\varepsilon^{j}e^{-sh^{\frac{Y-2}{Y}}}\ll h^{\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{j-Y-1}
, for any integer
j
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big((\mp J_{h})^{b}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\varepsilon\mp J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)^{k}\phi\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=(\sigma\sqrt{h})^{-k}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{k}\binom{k}{m}\varepsilon^{k-m}(\mp J_{h})^{m+b}\,\phi\left(\frac{\varepsilon\pm J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)^{k}\sum_{m=0}^{k}\binom{k}{m}\varepsilon^{-m}\,O\left(h^{3/2}\varepsilon^{m+b-Y-1}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(h^{\frac{3-k}{2}}\varepsilon^{k+b-Y-1}\right).

(196)

Moreover, using Lemma
17
, we see that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(J_{h}^{b}\,\overline{\Phi}\left(\frac{-\varepsilon-J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\right)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(J_{h}^{b}\,\overline{\Phi}\left(\frac{\varepsilon-J_{h}}{\sigma\sqrt{h}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(J_{h}^{b}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)=\begin{cases}O(h\varepsilon^{b-Y}),&b\text{ even},\\
O(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{b-Y/2}),&b\text{ odd}.\end{cases}

(197)

where we used Cauchy-Schwarz for the case when
b
is odd. Thus, by conditioning on
J_{h}
, we may use the bounds (
196
) and (
197
) in expresssion (
195
) to obtain

T_{1}(h)=\begin{cases}O\left(h^{2-a/2}\varepsilon^{a+b-Y-2}\right),&a\text{ odd},\\
O\left(h^{2-a/2}\varepsilon^{a+b-Y-2}\right)+O(h\varepsilon^{b-Y}),&a,b\text{ even},\\
O\left(h^{2-a/2}\varepsilon^{a+b-Y-2}\right)+O(h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{b-Y/2}),&a\text{ even},b\text{ odd}.\end{cases}

(198)

Now we turn to
T_{2}(h)
. Recall that by (
177
),
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\big)^{2}=O(h)
as
h\rightarrow 0
.
Then by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle T_{2}(h)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\big)\,W_{1}^{a}\,J_{h}^{b}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(W^{2a}_{1}\big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\big)^{2}\,\right)\right)^{1/2}\cdot\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(J_{h}^{2b}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma\sqrt{h}W_{1}+J_{h}|\leq\varepsilon\}}\right)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1\big)^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(O\big(h\varepsilon^{2b-Y}\big)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=O\big(h\varepsilon^{b-Y/2}\big),

(199)

where we used the independence of
W_{1}
and
U_{t}
in the third line above.
Therefore, since
h\varepsilon^{b-Y/2}\ll h\varepsilon^{b-Y}
and
h\varepsilon^{b-Y/2}\ll h^{1/2}\varepsilon^{b-Y/2}
, by (
194
), (
198
) and (
199
), the result holds.
∎

Proof of (
89
).

Let us start with the decomposition

\displaystyle I_{5,2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1+\widetilde{U}_{h}\Big)S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\widetilde{U}_{h}S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=:T_{1}-T_{2}.

Since
|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon
implies that
|S_{h}|<\varepsilon+\sigma|W_{h}|+|\widetilde{\gamma}|h
and
e^{-x}-1+x\geq{}0
for all
x
, we can estimate the first term as follows:

\displaystyle 0\leq{}T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq{}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\left(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1+\widetilde{U}_{h}\right)\left(\varepsilon+\sigma|W_{h}|+|\widetilde{\gamma}|h\right)^{2}\Big)

(200)

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{2})\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1+\widetilde{U}_{h}\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{2}h).

For
T_{2}
, we consider the following decomposition

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{h}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{h}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}}\big(\varphi(x)+\alpha_{\text{sgn}(x)}x{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}}\big)\widetilde{N}(ds,dx)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\int_{0}^{h}\int_{0<|x|<1}\alpha_{\text{sgn}(x)}x\widetilde{N}(ds,dx)

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{U}^{\text{BV}}_{h}-S^{(0)}_{t},

where the first integral is well-defined in light of Assumption
1
-(i) & (ii). Then, we have

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\widetilde{U}^{BV}_{h}S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{(0)}S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big).

(201)

For the first term we proceed as in (
208
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(

\displaystyle|\widetilde{U}^{BV}_{h}|S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|\widetilde{U}^{BV}_{h}|\left(\varepsilon+\sigma|W_{h}|+|\widetilde{\gamma}|h\right)^{2}\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{2})\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|\widetilde{U}^{BV}_{h}|\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{2}h)

(202)

since
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\big|\widetilde{U}^{\text{BV}}_{h}\big|\Big)\leq 2h\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}}\big|\varphi(x)+\alpha_{\text{sgn}(x)}x\big|\,\widetilde{\nu}(dx)<\infty
due to Assumption
1
.
For the expectations in (
209
), we apply Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|S_{h}^{(0)}|S_{h}^{2}\,

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

(203)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|S_{h}^{(0)}\right|^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}\bigg({\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)^{q}\bigg)^{1/q},

where
p,q>1
are such that
p^{-1}+q^{-1}=1
. However, by Lemma
16
,

\displaystyle\bigg({\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=\Big(O\big(h\varepsilon^{2q-Y}\big)\Big)^{1/q}=O\big(h^{1/q}\varepsilon^{2-Y/q}\big).

Therefore, plugging the above estimate into expression (
211
), and since
\mathbb{E}\big|S_{h}^{(0)}\big|^{p}\leq Ch
, we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|S_{h}^{(0)}|S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq O(h^{1/p})\times O\big(h^{1/q}\varepsilon^{2-Y/q})

\displaystyle=O(h\varepsilon^{2-Y/q}).

Therefore, for all small
\bar{\delta}>0
, by taking
q
large enough,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|S_{h}^{(0)}|S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}}).

(204)

Finally, combining (
208
), (
210
), and (
212
), we get that

\displaystyle I_{5,2}=O(\varepsilon^{2}h)+O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}})=O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}}).

(205)

∎

The following lemma is used in the proof of Lemma
16
.

Lemma 20
.

Suppose
Y\in(1,2)
, and let
\mathcal{D}(u)
be as in (
165
).Then,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}u^{2}\mathcal{D}(u)du=0.

(206)

Proof.

Let
\varphi_{S}(\omega)
denote the characteristic function of
S_{1}
under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
. Then, with
\varsigma
and
\rho
denoting the scale and skewness parameters of
S_{1}
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{S}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(-|\varsigma\omega|^{Y}\big[1-i\rho\hskip 1.42262pt{\rm sgn}(\omega)\tan(\pi Y/2)\big]\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\!\Bigg(\!-(C_{+}\!+C_{-})\,\bigg|\!\cos\bigg(\!\frac{\pi Y}{2}\!\bigg)\!\bigg|\Gamma(-Y)|\omega|^{Y}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigg[\!1-i\frac{C_{+}\!-C_{-}}{C_{+}\!+C_{-}}\tan\!\bigg(\!\frac{\pi Y}{2}\!\bigg)\text{sgn}(\omega)\!\bigg]\!\Bigg).

Without loss of generality, suppose
\varsigma=1.

For simplicity, let
\mathcal{E}(u):=u^{2}\mathcal{D}(u)
, and observe
\mathcal{E}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
since
Y\in(1,2)
, and hence
\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\omega):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{i\omega u}\mathcal{E}(u)du
exists.
Now, recall that, for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{ix}x^{\alpha-1}dx=e^{i\pi\alpha/2}\Gamma(\alpha),

(see
Gradshteyn and Ryzhik 2007
, eq. 3.381.7). Thus,

\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{iu\omega}u^{1-Y}du=-|\omega|^{Y-2}e^{-\text{sgn}(\omega)i\pi Y/2}\Gamma(2-Y),\quad Y\in(1,2).

(207)

This implies

\displaystyle C_{+}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{ix\omega}|x|^{1-Y}dx+C_{-}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{ix\omega}|x|^{1-Y}dx

\displaystyle=-|\omega|^{Y-2}\,\Gamma(2-Y)\left(C_{+}e^{-i\,\text{sgn}(\omega)\pi Y/2}+C_{-}e^{i\,\text{sgn}(\omega)\pi Y/2}\right)

\displaystyle=|\omega|^{Y-2}(C_{+}\!+C_{-})\,\Big|\cos\bigg(\!\frac{\pi Y}{2}\!\bigg)\Big|\Gamma(2-Y)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\Big[\!1-i\frac{C_{+}\!-C_{-}}{C_{+}\!+C_{-}}\tan\!\bigg(\!\frac{\pi Y}{2}\!\bigg)\text{sgn}(\omega)\!\Big]

\displaystyle=|\omega|^{Y-2}Y(Y-1)\Big(1-i\rho\hskip 1.42262pt{\rm sgn}(\omega)\tan(\pi Y/2)\Big).

However, it can be shown that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{iu\omega}u^{2}p_{S}(u)du=-\varphi_{S}^{\prime\prime}(\omega),\quad\omega\neq 0,

where the integral is interpreted as the limit
\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{-R}^{R}e^{iu\omega}u^{2}p_{S}(u)du.
Thus, by differentiating
-\varphi_{S}(\omega)
twice, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{iu\omega}

\displaystyle u^{2}p_{S}(u)du

\displaystyle=\Big(Y(Y-1)|\omega|^{Y-2}-Y^{2}|\omega|^{2Y-2}\Big)\Big(1-i\rho\hskip 1.42262pt{\rm sgn}(\omega)\tan(\pi Y/2)\Big)\varphi_{S}(\omega).

Therefore, as
\omega\to 0,

\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\omega)=-\hat{p}^{\prime\prime}(\omega)+|\omega|^{Y-2}Y(Y-1)\left(1-i\rho\hskip 1.42262pt{\rm sgn}(\omega)\tan(\pi Y/2)\right)=O\left(|\omega|^{2Y-2}\right).

In particular,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{E}(x)dx=\hat{\mathcal{E}}(0)=0,\quad Y\in(1,2).

∎

Proof of (
89
).

Let us start with the decomposition

\displaystyle I_{5,2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1+\widetilde{U}_{h}\Big)S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\widetilde{U}_{h}S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=:T_{1}-T_{2}.

Since
|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon
implies that
|S_{h}|<\varepsilon+\sigma|W_{h}|+|\widetilde{\gamma}|h
and
e^{-x}-1+x\geq{}0
for all
x
, we can estimate the first term as follows:

\displaystyle 0\leq{}T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq{}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\left(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1+\widetilde{U}_{h}\right)\left(\varepsilon+\sigma|W_{h}|+|\widetilde{\gamma}|h\right)^{2}\Big)

(208)

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{2})\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(e^{-\widetilde{U}_{h}}-1+\widetilde{U}_{h}\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{2}h).

For
T_{2}
, we consider the following decomposition

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{h}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{h}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}}\big(\varphi(x)+\alpha_{\text{sgn}(x)}x{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}}\big)\widetilde{N}(ds,dx)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\int_{0}^{h}\int_{0<|x|<1}\alpha_{\text{sgn}(x)}x\widetilde{N}(ds,dx)

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{U}^{\text{BV}}_{h}-S^{(0)}_{t},

where the first integral is well-defined in light of Assumption
1
-(i) & (ii). Then, we have

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\widetilde{U}^{BV}_{h}S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(S_{h}^{(0)}S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big).

(209)

For the first term we proceed as in (
208
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(

\displaystyle|\widetilde{U}^{BV}_{h}|S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|\widetilde{U}^{BV}_{h}|\left(\varepsilon+\sigma|W_{h}|+|\widetilde{\gamma}|h\right)^{2}\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{2})\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|\widetilde{U}^{BV}_{h}|\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{2}h)

(210)

since
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\big|\widetilde{U}^{\text{BV}}_{h}\big|\Big)\leq 2h\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}}\big|\varphi(x)+\alpha_{\text{sgn}(x)}x\big|\,\widetilde{\nu}(dx)<\infty
due to Assumption
1
.
For the expectations in (
209
), we apply Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|S_{h}^{(0)}|S_{h}^{2}\,

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

(211)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|S_{h}^{(0)}\right|^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}\bigg({\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)^{q}\bigg)^{1/q},

where
p,q>1
are such that
p^{-1}+q^{-1}=1
. However, by Lemma
16
,

\displaystyle\bigg({\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\Big(S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=\Big(O\big(h\varepsilon^{2q-Y}\big)\Big)^{1/q}=O\big(h^{1/q}\varepsilon^{2-Y/q}\big).

Therefore, plugging the above estimate into expression (
211
), and since
\mathbb{E}\big|S_{h}^{(0)}\big|^{p}\leq Ch
, we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|S_{h}^{(0)}|S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq O(h^{1/p})\times O\big(h^{1/q}\varepsilon^{2-Y/q})

\displaystyle=O(h\varepsilon^{2-Y/q}).

Therefore, for all small
\bar{\delta}>0
, by taking
q
large enough,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big(|S_{h}^{(0)}|S_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma W_{h}+S_{h}+\widetilde{\gamma}h|\leq\varepsilon\}}\Big)

\displaystyle=O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}}).

(212)

Finally, combining (
208
), (
210
), and (
212
), we get that

\displaystyle I_{5,2}=O(\varepsilon^{2}h)+O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}})=O(h\varepsilon^{2-\bar{\delta}}).

(213)

∎

Proof of (
129
).

First, we note that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J|>4\zeta\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle|f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)-f_{n}(\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J)||\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\Big]=o_{P}(1),

since, recalling notation (
97
)-(
98
),

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J|>4\zeta\varepsilon\}}|f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)-f_{n}(\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J)|

\displaystyle={\bf 1}_{\{|\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J|>4\zeta\varepsilon\}}f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)

\displaystyle\leq{}{\bf 1}_{\{|b_{t_{i-1}}h+\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W+\mathcal{E}_{i}|>3\zeta\varepsilon\}},

and Markov’s inequality together with
\varepsilon\gg h^{\beta}
and (
99
) for
r
large enough will easily show that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}({\bf 1}_{\{|b_{t_{i-1}}h>\zeta\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W|>\zeta\varepsilon\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{|\mathcal{E}_{i}|>\zeta\varepsilon\}})\right]=o_{P}(1)

So, we only need to prove that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{|\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}4\zeta\varepsilon\}}

\displaystyle|f_{n}(\Delta_{i}^{n}X)-f_{n}(\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J)||\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\Big]=o_{P}(1).

We will make use of the following bound

|f_{n}(x+y)-f_{n}(y)|\leq{}{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq{}\frac{1}{3}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}+{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq{}|y|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\;{\text{o}r}\;\zeta\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon^{\eta})\leq{}|y|\leq{}\zeta\varepsilon\}}\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon^{1+\eta}},

(214)

with
y=\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J
and
x=\Delta_{i}^{n}X-\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J=b_{t_{i-1}}h+\sigma_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}W+\mathcal{E}_{i}=:z_{i,1}+z_{i,2}+z_{i,3}
. It is easy to check that

A_{\ell}:=\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}4\zeta\varepsilon\}}{\bf 1}_{\{|z_{i,\ell}|\geq{}\frac{1}{9}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\}}|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\right]=o_{P}(1),

for
\ell=1,2,3;
for instance, consider
\ell=2
. Assuming for simplicity
\chi_{t_{i-1}}=\sigma_{t_{i-1}}=1
,

\displaystyle A_{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}

\displaystyle\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{8}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}4\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\geq{}\frac{1}{9}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For any
\alpha>0
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|\geq{}\frac{1}{9}\varepsilon^{1+\eta}\right]\leq{}C\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}W|^{\alpha}\right]/\varepsilon^{\alpha(1+\eta)}\leq{}Ch^{\alpha/2}/\varepsilon^{\alpha(1+\eta)}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{4}}\varepsilon^{\frac{8-Y}{4}}h^{\frac{\alpha}{8}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{\alpha(1+\eta)}{4}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y}{4}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}\big(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-(1+\eta)})^{\frac{\alpha}{4}}O_{P}(1).

Recalling that
\varepsilon\gg h^{\beta}
, for small enough
\eta>0
,
\frac{1}{2}-\beta(1+\eta)>0
, hence taking
\alpha
large enough gives
A_{2}=o_{P}(1).
The argument for
A_{1}
is similar. For
A_{3}
,

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{8}{\bf 1}_{\{|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}4\zeta\varepsilon\}}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\mathcal{E}_{i}|^{\alpha}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha(1+\eta)}{4}}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{}C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{4}}\varepsilon^{\frac{8-Y}{4}}h^{\frac{1+\alpha/2}{4}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{\alpha(1+\eta)}{4}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{Y}{4}}h^{\frac{\alpha}{8}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{\alpha(1+\eta)}{4}}O_{P}(1).

proceeding as in the case of
A_{2}
, the above tends to 0 provided
\alpha
is large enough and
\eta
is sufficiently small.
Next, we analyze the second term in (
214
). We consider only
\{\varepsilon\leq{}|y|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\}
(the other case is similar). In other words, we need to show that

B_{\ell}:=\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}-1-\eta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq{}|\chi_{t_{i-1}}\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}|z_{i,\ell}||\Delta_{i}^{n}M|\right]=o_{P}(1),

for
\ell=1,2,3.
Assume for simplicity of notation that
\chi_{t_{i-1}}=\sigma_{t_{i-1}}=1
.
By Cauchy’s inequality and Lemma
17
, for any
p,q>0
such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}
,

\displaystyle B_{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}-1-\eta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\Big[\Delta_{i}^{n}J^{2p}

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{\varepsilon\leq{}|\Delta_{i}^{n}J|\leq{}\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon^{\eta})\}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}W|^{q}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}-1-\eta}\left(h\varepsilon^{2p-Y+\eta}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{i-1}\left[|\Delta_{i}^{n}M|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}-1-\eta}\left(h\varepsilon^{2p-Y+\eta}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}O_{P}(1)

(c.f. (
136
).) Taking
p
close to 2 (so
\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{2}-s
for
s>0
small), the last expression is of order
\varepsilon^{\frac{Y-4}{2}-1-\eta}h^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\varepsilon^{2-\frac{Y}{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}+s(Y-\eta)}=h^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\varepsilon^{\frac{\eta}{2}-1+s(Y-\eta)}\leq h^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\varepsilon^{\frac{\eta}{2}-1}
. Using that
\varepsilon\gg h^{\beta}
,

h^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\varepsilon^{\frac{\eta}{2}-1}\ll h^{\frac{1}{2}-s-(1-\frac{\eta}{2})\beta}\ll 1,

if
\eta
and
s
are small enough. Similar arguments give
B_{1}=o_{P}(1)
and
B_{3}=o_{P}(1)
, which establishes (
129
).
∎
